Response: To take this from a Bash script on Fedora to a production-grade, cross-platform CLI that runs seamlessly on Debian/Ubuntu, Arch, Fedora, and Windows, Bash will no longer cut it (Windows cmd/PowerShell compatibility and platform-agnostic process execution would quickly turn Bash into an unmaintainable wrapper).

Here is the proposed architectural blueprint, breakdown of core features, plugin design, and installer mechanics.

### **Core Architectural Pivot: Binary Runtime**

Instead of a shell script, build the CLI core in **Go** or **Rust** (or packaged Python via PyInstaller/uv):

> * Compiles down to a single, standalone binary across Linux (x86\_64, aarch64) and Windows (.exe).  
> * Uses native cross-platform process management (spawning background processes, handling SIGINT/Ctrl+C, managing child process timeouts) without relying on Linux-only utilities like sed, eval, or kill \-TERM.  
> * Built-in file exclusion logic to permanently solve recursive scanning bugs (e.g., auto-ignoring reports/, .git/, node\_modules/, target/).

### **Key Feature Specifications**

**1\. Dual-Engine LLM Provider (Local Ollama vs. BYO API Key)**

> * **Local Mode (Default):** Checks if Ollama is running; if not, handles auto-spinup and pulls your lightweight model (qwen2.5-coder:1.5b or user-specified model).  
> * **Cloud API Mode:** Prompts the user or reads environment variables/config file (\~/.codeaudit/config.yaml) for providers:  
  * OpenAI (gpt-4o, o3-mini)  
  * Anthropic (claude-3-7-sonnet)  
  * DeepSeek / OpenRouter / Groq (compatible via OpenAI-format REST endpoints).  
> * A unified prompt abstraction layer passes scanner diagnostics and diffs regardless of whether the model runs locally or over HTTPS.

**2\. Execution Modes (Report Only, Auto-Patch, or Both)**

> * **Audit & Report:** Runs scanners \+ LLM synthesis, producing detailed\_audit\_report\_\<ts\>.md, summary\_audit\_report\_\<ts\>.md, and updates timeline.md.  
> * **Remediate / Auto-Patch:**  
  * Parses LLM-generated unified diffs (diff \--git).  
  * Validates patch safety with a dry run (git apply \--check).  
  * Presents interactive diff approvals (accept all, review per-file, or reject).  
> * **Hybrid Mode:** Generates reports first, applies approved fixes directly to the working tree, and generates a post-fix timeline entry showing delta reductions.

**3\. Persona-Driven Audit Plugins** Rather than just a generic scan, the auditor switches analysis heuristics and system prompts based on the selected audit lens:

| Persona Plugin | Primary Focus & Rule Sets | Specific Checks Performed |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Software Engineer** | Architecture, clean code, maintainability | Circular dependencies, tight coupling, memory/resource leaks, concurrency safety, unhandled errors |
| **Web Developer** | Web ecosystem security & OWASP Top 10 | SSRF, XSS vectors, CSRF, insecure CORS, JWT misconfigurations, prototype pollution, exposed API keys |
| **AI / ML Engineer** | Model security, data pipelines, eval safety | Prompt injection vectors, insecure model deserialization (pickle, unverified safe tensors), API rate limits, data leakage |
| **Cybersecurity / Red Team** | Offensive attack surface & exploitation vectors | Hardcoded credentials, unpinned CI actions, binary exploit pathways, privilege escalation risks, vulnerable dependency trees |

### **Universal One-Line Installer Plan**

A bootstrap script detecting OS, package manager, and platform architecture:

> * **Linux:** curl \-fsSL https://.../install.sh | bash  
  * Detects apt (Debian/Ubuntu), dnf (Fedora/RHEL), or pacman (Arch).  
  * Automatically installs missing foundation runtimes if needed: gcc/g++, python3, node/npm, cargo/rust.  
  * Installs audit tools: gitleaks, semgrep, trivy, ruff, biome.  
  * Places the auditor binary in /usr/local/bin or \~/.local/bin.  
> * **Windows:** irm https://.../install.ps1 | iex  
  * Checks for winget or choco to install Git, Python, Node, and security scanners.  
  * Registers binary path in user Environment Variables.

### **Recommended Enhancements to Consider**

> 1. **Deterministic Auto-Fixes vs. LLM Auto-Fixes:**  
   * Run deterministic tool fixes *first* (ruff check \--fix, biome format \--write, cargo fix \--allow-no-vcs) before asking the LLM to write code. This saves token compute and prevents hallucinated syntax errors.  
> 2. **Git Branch Isolation for Auto-Fixes:**  
   * When the user chooses to "Fix Vulnerabilities", have the tool automatically offer to create a new branch (e.g., audit-fix-YYYYMMDD) rather than writing directly to main or the active working tree.  
> 3. **Interactive TUI (Terminal User Interface):**  
   * Use a terminal interface library (like bubbletea in Go or ratatui in Rust) so the user can easily toggle plugins (Web Dev, Red Team, AI/ML) using spacebars and arrow keys instead of complex text menus.

Response: Building the auditor in **Rust** gives you zero-runtime overhead, strict memory safety, and cross-platform native binaries for Linux and Windows.

Here are the additional persona plugins and architectural refinements to complete the plan before you begin writing code.

### **Recommended Additional Persona Plugins**

**1\. DevOps & Cloud Infrastructure (SRE / Platform Engineer)**

> * **Why it matters:** Most modern vulnerabilities occur in CI/CD configuration, deployment manifests, and container setups rather than application logic (e.g., the unpinned GitHub Actions and secrets: inherit flagged in your previous scans).  
> * **Checks performed:**  
  * **CI/CD Security:** Mutable workflow action tags, overprivileged tokens (GITHUB\_TOKEN write permissions), pipeline command injections.  
  * **Containers & Orchestration:** Root user in Dockerfiles, missing security contexts in Kubernetes manifests, exposed daemon sockets.  
  * **IaC Flaws:** Public S3 buckets, overly permissive security groups in Terraform/OpenTofu files.

**2\. Legal & OSS Compliance (Open-Source Auditor)**

> * **Why it matters:** Commercial codebases frequently violate open-source licensing through accidental viral copyleft contamination (e.g., linking GPL-3.0 code into closed-source or MIT-licensed proprietary repositories).  
> * **Checks performed:**  
  * Dependency license conflict detection (MIT vs. GPL vs. AGPL vs. Apache 2.0).  
  * Missing attribution files or missing LICENSE headers in source code.

**3\. Data & Database Engineer**

> * **Why it matters:** Web developers often catch basic SQL injections, but miss catastrophic database-level anti-patterns that take production down.  
> * **Checks performed:**  
  * Hardcoded connection URIs and unencrypted socket connections.  
  * Schema anti-patterns, ORM N+1 query traps, and unsafe dynamic query concatenation.

### **Critical Rust Architecture Recommendations**

**1\. Modular Workspace Layout** Split the Rust project into focused internal crates to keep compilation times fast and tests isolated:

> * crates/core: Shared models (Finding, Severity, Persona, ReportMeta).  
> * crates/scanners: Process runners and stdout/JSON parsers for Gitleaks, Semgrep, Trivy, Biome, Clippy, and Ruff.  
> * crates/llm: Unified HTTP client (reqwest) supporting both Ollama's local endpoints (/api/generate) and standard OpenAI-compatible cloud endpoints (/v1/chat/completions).  
> * crates/remediator: Deterministic tool fix dispatchers (cargo fix, ruff \--fix), branch creation via native git2, and patch application validation (git apply \--check).  
> * crates/tui: Terminal User Interface built with ratatui and crossterm.  
> * crates/cli: Entry point binary and clap CLI flags.

**2\. Structured JSON Over Raw Regex Parsing**

> * Instead of capturing raw CLI stdout and parsing it with shell regexes (which break between tool version updates), invoke tools with their native JSON flags:  
  * Semgrep: semgrep scan \--json  
  * Gitleaks: gitleaks detect \--report-format json \--report-path \<tmp\>  
  * Trivy: trivy fs \--format json  
  * Ruff: ruff check \--output-format json  
  * Biome: biome check \--reporter json  
> * Deserialize all outputs into a strongly typed Vec\<RawFinding\> using serde\_json, allowing the LLM prompt builder to construct token-efficient summaries rather than dumping hundreds of noisy terminal lines.

**3\. Two-Tier Binary Distribution in Installer**

> * If the one-line installer has to build the tool via cargo install \--path ., users without pre-configured Rust toolchains will experience 10+ minute build times.  
> * Configure a GitHub Actions CI workflow to cross-compile and publish release tarballs/zips (x86\_64-unknown-linux-gnu, aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu, x86\_64-pc-windows-msvc).  
> * The installer script simply downloads the precompiled binary directly into the user's PATH, only compiling from source as an explicit fallback.

**4\. Project-Level Config (.codeaudit.toml)**

> * Allow projects to check in an optional .codeaudit.toml at their root directory:  
  * Defines custom paths to ignore (automatically ignoring previous reports/ so Gitleaks never flags old reports again).  
  * Pins default persona selections for that repository.  
  * Specifies whether cloud APIs or local Ollama instances take precedence.

Response: Here is the complete architectural layout, dependency blueprint, and the primary CLI interface design for your Rust-based codebase auditor.

### **1\. Cargo Workspace Architecture**

We structure this as a multi-crate Cargo workspace to decouple the UI, external scanners, remediation logic, and AI interfaces:

codeaudit/  
├── Cargo.toml                  \# Workspace root manifest  
├── crates/  
│   ├── core/                   \# Shared data types, traits, and configurations  
│   ├── scanners/               \# Process dispatchers & structured JSON parsers  
│   ├── llm/                    \# Unified client (Ollama local \+ OpenAI/Anthropic/DeepSeek)  
│   ├── remediator/             \# Git branch manager, deterministic tool fixes, patch engine  
│   ├── tui/                    \# Terminal User Interface (Ratatui / Crossterm)  
│   └── cli/                    \# Main binary entry point, CLI arguments (Clap)  
└── scripts/  
    ├── install.sh              \# Universal Linux installer (Debian, Arch, Fedora)  
    └── install.ps1             \# Universal Windows installer

### **2\. Core Dependencies Specification**

#### **Root Cargo.toml**

\[workspace\]  
resolver \= "2"  
members \= \[  
    "crates/core",  
    "crates/scanners",  
    "crates/llm",  
    "crates/remediator",  
    "crates/tui",  
    "crates/cli",  
\]

\[workspace.package\]  
version \= "0.1.0"  
edition \= "2021"  
authors \= \["Your Name \<you@example.com\>"\]  
license \= "MIT OR Apache-2.0"

\[workspace.dependencies\]  
\# Serialization & Data  
serde \= { version \= "1.0", features \= \["derive"\] }  
serde\_json \= "1.0"  
serde\_yaml \= "0.9"  
toml \= "0.8"

\# Async & Process Management  
tokio \= { version \= "1.40", features \= \["full"\] }  
async-trait \= "0.1"  
futures \= "0.3"

\# CLI & Interactive Terminal  
clap \= { version \= "4.5", features \= \["derive", "env"\] }  
ratatui \= "0.29"  
crossterm \= "0.28"  
indicatif \= { version \= "0.17", features \= \["tokio"\] }  
colored \= "2.1"  
dialoguer \= "0.11"

\# HTTP & APIs  
reqwest \= { version \= "0.12", default-features \= false, features \= \["json", "rustls-tls", "stream"\] }

\# Git & File Handling  
git2 \= "0.19"  
walkdir \= "2.5"  
ignore \= "0.4"  
tempfile \= "3.12"  
diffy \= "0.4"

\# Error Handling & Utilities  
thiserror \= "1.0"  
anyhow \= "1.0"  
chrono \= { version \= "0.4", features \= \["serde"\] }  
tracing \= "0.1"  
tracing-subscriber \= "0.3"

### **3\. CLI Design & Command Structure**

The CLI supports two modes:

> 1. **Interactive Guided Mode:** Launching with codeaudit (or codeaudit gui) drops into an interactive TUI to pick personas, review findings, and approve patches.  
> 2. **Headless / CI Mode:** Passing flags directly (codeaudit scan \--persona red-team \--fix) bypasses interactive prompts for CI/CD automation.

#### **Defining the Command-Line Interface (crates/cli/src/main.rs)**

use clap::{Parser, Subcommand, ValueEnum};  
use std::path::PathBuf;

\#\[derive(Parser, Debug)\]  
\#\[command(  
    name \= "codeaudit",  
    version \= "0.1.0",  
    about \= "Production-grade, cross-platform codebase security & quality auditor",  
    long\_about \= None  
)\]  
pub struct Cli {  
    \#\[arg(short, long, global \= true, help \= "Path to the target codebase \[default: current dir\]")\]  
    pub target: Option\<PathBuf\>,

    \#\[arg(short, long, global \= true, help \= "Path to write audit reports \[default: \<target\>/reports\]")\]  
    pub reports\_dir: Option\<PathBuf\>,

    \#\[arg(long, global \= true, help \= "Path to a custom .codeaudit.toml config")\]  
    pub config: Option\<PathBuf\>,

    \#\[command(subcommand)\]  
    pub command: Option\<Commands\>,  
}

\#\[derive(Subcommand, Debug)\]  
pub enum Commands {  
    \#\[command(about \= "Run the full audit pipeline")\]  
    Scan(ScanArgs),

    \#\[command(about \= "Launch the interactive Terminal User Interface (TUI)")\]  
    Tui,

    \#\[command(about \= "Manage and verify toolchain dependencies (Gitleaks, Semgrep, Trivy, etc.)")\]  
    Doctor,

    \#\[command(about \= "Configure default AI providers, API keys, and model parameters")\]  
    Config,  
}

\#\[derive(Parser, Debug)\]  
pub struct ScanArgs {  
    \#\[arg(  
        short,   
        long,   
        value\_delimiter \= ',',   
        help \= "Active persona plugins: swe, web, ai-ml, red-team, devops, oss-compliance, database (or 'all')"  
    )\]  
    pub personas: Vec\<PersonaArg\>,

    \#\[arg(long, value\_enum, default\_value\_t \= ProviderMode::LocalOllama, help \= "AI Engine to use")\]  
    pub provider: ProviderMode,

    \#\[arg(long, help \= "Explicit model name (e.g. qwen2.5-coder:1.5b, gpt-4o, claude-3-7-sonnet)")\]  
    pub model: Option\<String\>,

    \#\[arg(long, help \= "API key for cloud-based providers (can also be read via environment variables)")\]  
    pub api\_key: Option\<String\>,

    \#\[arg(long, value\_enum, default\_value\_t \= ActionOutcome::Both, help \= "What action to take on findings")\]  
    pub action: ActionOutcome,

    \#\[arg(long, help \= "Name of git branch to create if auto-remediation is executed")\]  
    pub fix\_branch: Option\<String\>,

    \#\[arg(long, default\_value\_t \= false, help \= "Skip deterministic linters (clippy, ruff, biome)")\]  
    pub skip\_linters: bool,

    \#\[arg(long, default\_value\_t \= false, help \= "Skip AI generation and only produce raw scanner summaries")\]  
    pub raw\_only: bool,  
}

\#\[derive(ValueEnum, Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)\]  
pub enum PersonaArg {  
    All,  
    Swe,  
    Web,  
    AiMl,  
    RedTeam,  
    Devops,  
    OssCompliance,  
    Database,  
}

\#\[derive(ValueEnum, Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)\]  
pub enum ProviderMode {  
    LocalOllama,  
    Openai,  
    Anthropic,  
    Deepseek,  
    Openrouter,  
}

\#\[derive(ValueEnum, Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)\]  
pub enum ActionOutcome {  
    ReportOnly,  
    FixOnly,  
    Both,  
}

### **4\. Core Domain Models (crates/core/src/models.rs)**

This structure represents parsed scanner outputs, AI prompts, and remediation tasks:

use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};  
use std::path::PathBuf;

\#\[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize, PartialEq, Eq)\]  
pub enum Severity {  
    Critical,  
    High,  
    Medium,  
    Low,  
    Info,  
}

\#\[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)\]  
pub enum Persona {  
    SoftwareEngineer,  
    WebDeveloper,  
    AiMlEngineer,  
    RedTeamCybersecurity,  
    DevOpsPlatform,  
    OssCompliance,  
    DatabaseEngineer,  
}

\#\[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)\]  
pub struct UnifiedFinding {  
    pub id: String,  
    pub source: ScannerSource,  
    pub rule\_id: String,  
    pub severity: Severity,  
    pub message: String,  
    pub file\_path: PathBuf,  
    pub line\_start: usize,  
    pub line\_end: Option\<usize\>,  
    pub matched\_content: Option\<String\>,  
    pub fixable: bool,  
    pub suggested\_patch: Option\<String\>,  
}

\#\[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize, PartialEq, Eq)\]  
pub enum ScannerSource {  
    Gitleaks,  
    Semgrep,  
    Trivy,  
    Clippy,  
    Ruff,  
    Biome,  
    AiGenerated(Persona),  
}

\#\[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)\]  
pub struct AuditRunStats {  
    pub timestamp: chrono::DateTime\<chrono::Utc\>,  
    pub total\_secrets: usize,  
    pub total\_sast: usize,  
    pub total\_cves: usize,  
    pub total\_linter\_issues: usize,  
    pub issues\_fixed: usize,  
}

### **Additional Recommendations Before Implementation**

> 1. **Native AST File Chunking (Tree-sitter):**  
   * Currently, the script takes the top 80 lines of a few files.  
   * Using the tree-sitter crate allows you to only send the **body of the function or block that contains the linter/SAST finding** to the LLM. This cuts down token usage by 80% and fits within the smaller context windows of sub-4B local models.  
> 2. **Deterministic Pre-flight Sandbox (git stash / git checkout \-b):**  
   * Before running any automatic fixes, perform a quick Git check. If the working tree is dirty, either require a commit, stash uncommitted work automatically, or checkout the audit branch first so no active edits are destroyed.  
> 3. **Doctor Command:**  
   * Provide codeaudit doctor out of the box to scan the system PATH and print a green checkmark or yellow warning for every tool (Gitleaks, Semgrep, Trivy, Biome, Ruff, Cargo, Ollama), pointing users to automated installation commands for missing tools.

Response: Here are distinct naming directions grouped by the identity and vibe of the tool:

**Cybersecurity & Sentinel Themes**

> * **Vigilant** / **Vigilis** — Latin for the night watch; conveys continuous background surveillance and guarding against intrusions.  
> * **AegisCode** (or **AegisCLI**) — Evoking the mythological shield; suggests an unbreakable defensive barrier for repos.  
> * **Sentrak** (Sentinel \+ Track) — Sharp, modern, and memorable; sounds like a high-grade security appliance.  
> * **Wardstone** — Grounded and durable; suggests placing a protective perimeter ward around your repository before deployment.

**Auditing, Scanning & Precision Themes**

> * **TraceHound** — Relentless and methodical; fits a tool that traces data flows, secrets, and AST branches down to the exact root cause.  
> * **SpecterScan** — Technical and slightly covert; evokes a deep, unobtrusive background auditor that uncovers hidden flaws.  
> * **ScanThru** — Lightweight, punchy, and modern; feels fast and frictionless.  
> * **Veriduct** (Verdict \+ Duct/Audit) — Conveys delivering a final, trusted verdict on production readiness.

**Developer-Native & Minimalist Themes**

> * **HatchCheck** — Clean and developer-friendly; sounds like a security pre-flight check right before shipping code through the hatch.  
> * **CodeProwl** — Dynamic and agile; suggests actively hunting down bugs and vulnerabilities lurking across multiple directories.  
> * **ReviseAI** / **Revisor** — Professional and direct; frames the tool as an automated peer reviewer that drafts fixes rather than just complaining about errors.  
> * **Auditron** — Retro-tech, distinctive, and memorable; feels like an automated testing robot living inside the terminal.

**Persona & Multi-Perspective Themes**

> * **PrismCode** (or **PrismCLI**) — Like light splitting through a prism, the auditor breaks a codebase into multiple distinct lenses (SWE, Red Team, DevOps, ML).  
> * **PolyWatch** — Highlights the multi-language, multi-linter, and multi-persona nature of the engine.  
> * **Locus** — Sleek, modern, and focused; highlights pinpointing the exact coordinates of defects.

Which of these directions resonates best with what you want to brand the project around? Once you select your favorite (or a variation of one), we can lock it into the CLI binary name, Cargo package configuration, and universal install scripts.

---

*User prompt: The name I've decided is Code-Warden*

Response: **Code-Warden** (command: code-warden) fits the project's identity: a watchful guardian vetting the perimeter before production deployment.

Here are the universal installation scripts (install.sh for Linux and install.ps1 for Windows) along with the core parser module in crates/scanners to process structured JSON findings from Gitleaks, Semgrep, and Trivy.

### **1\. Universal Linux Installer (scripts/install.sh)**

This script handles Debian/Ubuntu (apt), Fedora/RHEL (dnf), and Arch Linux (pacman). It installs base runtimes, downloads precompiled scanner binaries, and registers code-warden in the system path.

\#\!/usr/bin/env bash  
set \-euo pipefail

BOLD='\\033\[1m'  
GREEN='\\033\[0;32m'  
BLUE='\\033\[0;34m'  
YELLOW='\\033\[1;33m'  
RED='\\033\[0;31m'  
NC='\\033\[0m'

BIN\_DIR="/usr/local/bin"  
REPO\_OWNER="your-github-username"  
REPO\_NAME="code-warden"

echo \-e "${BLUE}${BOLD}=== Installing Code-Warden & Core Security Toolchains \===${NC}"

\# Detect Package Manager  
detect\_pm() {  
    if command \-v apt-get \>/dev/null 2\>&1; then echo "apt";  
    elif command \-v dnf \>/dev/null 2\>&1; then echo "dnf";  
    elif command \-v pacman \>/dev/null 2\>&1; then echo "pacman";  
    else echo "unknown"; fi  
}

PM=$(detect\_pm)  
if \[ "$PM" \= "unknown" \]; then  
    echo \-e "${RED}\[X\] Unsupported Linux distribution. Please install dependencies manually.${NC}"  
    exit 1  
fi

echo \-e "${YELLOW}\[\*\] Detected package manager: ${PM}${NC}"  
echo \-e "${YELLOW}\[\*\] Requesting sudo permissions to configure system runtimes...${NC}"  
sudo \-v

\# Install Base Runtimes (C/C++, Python, Node, Git, Curl)  
echo \-e "\\n${BLUE}\[1/5\] Verifying foundation runtimes (C/C++, Python, Node, Git)...${NC}"  
case "$PM" in  
    apt)  
        sudo apt-get update \-qq  
        sudo apt-get install \-y \-qq build-essential curl git python3 python3-pip nodejs npm libssl-dev pkg-config  
        ;;  
    dnf)  
        sudo dnf groupinstall \-y "Development Tools" "C Development Tools and Libraries"  
        sudo dnf install \-y curl git python3 python3-pip nodejs npm openssl-devel  
        ;;  
    pacman)  
        sudo pacman \-Syu \--noconfirm \--needed base-devel curl git python python-pip nodejs npm openssl  
        ;;  
esac

\# Install Python & JS/TS Linters  
echo \-e "\\n${BLUE}\[2/5\] Installing native linters (Ruff, Biome)...${NC}"  
command \-v ruff \>/dev/null 2\>&1 || pip install \--quiet \--upgrade ruff || true  
command \-v biome \>/dev/null 2\>&1 || sudo npm install \-g @biomejs/biome \--silent || true

\# Install Security Scanners (Gitleaks, Semgrep, Trivy)  
echo \-e "\\n${BLUE}\[3/5\] Verifying security scanners (Gitleaks, Semgrep, Trivy)...${NC}"

\# Gitleaks  
if \! command \-v gitleaks \>/dev/null 2\>&1; then  
    echo \-e "${YELLOW}\[\*\] Installing Gitleaks...${NC}"  
    GL\_VER=$(curl \-s https://api.github.com/repos/gitleaks/gitleaks/releases/latest | grep '"tag\_name":' | sed \-E 's/.\*"v(\[^"\]+)".\*/\\1/')  
    ARCH=$(uname \-m)  
    \[ "$ARCH" \= "x86\_64" \] && GL\_ARCH="x64" || GL\_ARCH="arm64"  
    curl \-sSL "https://github.com/gitleaks/gitleaks/releases/download/v${GL\_VER}/gitleaks\_${GL\_VER}\_linux\_${GL\_ARCH}.tar.gz" | sudo tar \-xz \-C "$BIN\_DIR" gitleaks  
fi

\# Trivy  
if \! command \-v trivy \>/dev/null 2\>&1; then  
    echo \-e "${YELLOW}\[\*\] Installing Trivy...${NC}"  
    curl \-sfL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/aquasecurity/trivy/main/contrib/install.sh | sudo sh \-s \-- \-b "$BIN\_DIR"  
fi

\# Semgrep  
if \! command \-v semgrep \>/dev/null 2\>&1; then  
    echo \-e "${YELLOW}\[\*\] Installing Semgrep...${NC}"  
    pip install \--quiet \--upgrade semgrep || true  
fi

\# Ollama Engine Check  
echo \-e "\\n${BLUE}\[4/5\] Checking local AI engine (Ollama)...${NC}"  
if \! command \-v ollama \>/dev/null 2\>&1; then  
    echo \-e "${YELLOW}\[\*\] Installing Ollama...${NC}"  
    curl \-fsSL https://ollama.com/install.sh | sh  
fi

\# Fetch and Install Precompiled Code-Warden Binary  
echo \-e "\\n${BLUE}\[5/5\] Fetching Code-Warden CLI...${NC}"  
ARCH=$(uname \-m)  
case "$ARCH" in  
    x86\_64) TARGET="x86\_64-unknown-linux-gnu" ;;  
    aarch64) TARGET="aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu" ;;  
    \*) echo \-e "${RED}\[X\] Unsupported CPU architecture: $ARCH${NC}"; exit 1 ;;  
esac

ASSET\_URL="https://github.com/${REPO\_OWNER}/${REPO\_NAME}/releases/latest/download/code-warden-${TARGET}.tar.gz"  
if curl \--output /dev/null \--silent \--head \--fail "$ASSET\_URL"; then  
    echo \-e "${YELLOW}\[\*\] Downloading precompiled binary...${NC}"  
    curl \-sSL "$ASSET\_URL" | sudo tar \-xz \-C "$BIN\_DIR" code-warden  
else  
    echo \-e "${YELLOW}\[\!\] Precompiled binary not found on release channel. Compiling from source...${NC}"  
    command \-v cargo \>/dev/null 2\>&1 || { curl \--proto '=https' \--tlsv1.2 \-sSf https://sh.rustup.rs | sh \-s \-- \-y; source "$HOME/.cargo/env"; }  
    cargo install \--git "https://github.com/${REPO\_OWNER}/${REPO\_NAME}.git" \--bin code-warden \--root /usr/local  
fi

sudo chmod \+x "${BIN\_DIR}/code-warden"  
echo \-e "\\n${GREEN}${BOLD}\[✔\] Code-Warden installed successfully\! Run 'code-warden \--help' or 'code-warden tui' to start.${NC}"

### **2\. Universal Windows Installer (scripts/install.ps1)**

This script installs foundational packages via winget (or choco), configures the security scanners, pulls the latest binary, and updates the User PATH.

Write-Host "=== Installing Code-Warden for Windows \===" \-ForegroundColor Cyan

\# Elevate privileges if not admin  
if (-not (\[Security.Principal.WindowsPrincipal\]\[Security.Principal.WindowsIdentity\]::GetCurrent()).IsInRole(\[Security.Principal.WindowsBuiltInRole\]::Administrator)) {  
    Write-Host "\[\*\] Requesting Administrator Privileges..." \-ForegroundColor Yellow  
    Start-Process powershell.exe "-NoProfile \-ExecutionPolicy Bypass \-File \`"$PSCommandPath\`"" \-Verb RunAs  
    exit  
}

$InstallDir \= "$env:ProgramFiles\\Code-Warden"  
if (-not (Test-Path \-Path $InstallDir)) {  
    New-Item \-ItemType Directory \-Path $InstallDir | Out-Null  
}

\# Install Runtimes & Scanners via Winget  
Write-Host "\`n\[1/4\] Installing Required Runtimes & CLIs (Git, Python, Node, Ollama)..." \-ForegroundColor Cyan  
winget install \--id Git.Git \-e \--silent \--accept-source-agreements \--accept-package-agreements  
winget install \--id Python.Python.3.12 \-e \--silent \--accept-source-agreements \--accept-package-agreements  
winget install \--id OpenJS.NodeJS.LTS \-e \--silent \--accept-source-agreements \--accept-package-agreements  
winget install \--id Ollama.Ollama \-e \--silent \--accept-source-agreements \--accept-package-agreements

\# Install Linters and Scanners  
Write-Host "\`n\[2/4\] Installing Static Analyzers & Linters..." \-ForegroundColor Cyan  
pip install \--upgrade ruff semgrep | Out-Null  
npm install \-g @biomejs/biome \--silent | Out-Null

\# Gitleaks via Winget  
winget install \--id Gitleaks.Gitleaks \-e \--silent \--accept-source-agreements \--accept-package-agreements

\# Trivy via Winget  
winget install \--id AquaSecurity.Trivy \-e \--silent \--accept-source-agreements \--accept-package-agreements

\# Fetch Code-Warden Binary  
Write-Host "\`n\[3/4\] Downloading Code-Warden Executable..." \-ForegroundColor Cyan  
$Repo \= "your-github-username/code-warden"  
$AssetUrl \= "https://github.com/$Repo/releases/latest/download/code-warden-x86\_64-pc-windows-msvc.zip"  
$ZipPath \= "$env:TEMP\\code-warden.zip"

Invoke-WebRequest \-Uri $AssetUrl \-OutFile $ZipPath  
Expand-Archive \-Path $ZipPath \-DestinationPath $InstallDir \-Force  
Remove-Item \-Path $ZipPath

\# Persist PATH  
Write-Host "\`n\[4/4\] Configuring Environment PATH..." \-ForegroundColor Cyan  
$CurrentPath \= \[Environment\]::GetEnvironmentVariable("Path", \[EnvironmentVariableTarget\]::Machine)  
if ($CurrentPath \-notlike "\*$InstallDir\*") {  
    \[Environment\]::SetEnvironmentVariable("Path", "$CurrentPath;$InstallDir", \[EnvironmentVariableTarget\]::Machine)  
}

Write-Host "\`n\[✔\] Code-Warden installation complete\! Open a new terminal and run 'code-warden.exe'." \-ForegroundColor Green

### **3\. Structured Scanner Engine (crates/scanners/src/lib.rs)**

Instead of parsing fragile console outputs with regex, this module executes scanners directly and parses their native JSON schemas into UnifiedFinding models.

use anyhow::{Context, Result};  
use code\_warden\_core::models::{ScannerSource, Severity, UnifiedFinding};  
use serde\_json::Value;  
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};  
use std::process::Command;  
use tempfile::NamedTempFile;

pub struct ScannerEngine;

impl ScannerEngine {  
    /// Dispatches Gitleaks using \`--report-format json\`  
    pub fn scan\_gitleaks(target: \&Path) \-\> Result\<Vec\<UnifiedFinding\>\> {  
        let temp\_report \= NamedTempFile::new()?;  
        let report\_path \= temp\_report.path().to\_string\_lossy().to\_string();

        let output \= Command::new("gitleaks")  
            .args(\[  
                "detect",  
                "--source",  
                \&target.to\_string\_lossy(),  
                "--no-git",  
                "--report-format",  
                "json",  
                "--report-path",  
                \&report\_path,  
                "--redact",  
            \])  
            .output()  
            .context("Failed to execute gitleaks. Ensure it is installed and in PATH.")?;

        if \!Path::new(\&report\_path).exists() {  
            return Ok(Vec::new());  
        }

        let content \= std::fs::read\_to\_string(\&report\_path)?;  
        if content.trim().is\_empty() {  
            return Ok(Vec::new());  
        }

        let raw\_json: Value \= serde\_json::from\_str(\&content).unwrap\_or(Value::Array(vec\!\[\]));  
        let mut findings \= Vec::new();

        if let Some(leaks) \= raw\_json.as\_array() {  
            for item in leaks {  
                findings.push(UnifiedFinding {  
                    id: format\!("GITLEAKS-{}", uuid::Uuid::new\_v4()),  
                    source: ScannerSource::Gitleaks,  
                    rule\_id: item\["RuleID"\].as\_str().unwrap\_or("secret-leak").to\_string(),  
                    severity: Severity::Critical,  
                    message: item\["Description"\].as\_str().unwrap\_or("Secret leak detected").to\_string(),  
                    file\_path: PathBuf::from(item\["File"\].as\_str().unwrap\_or("unknown")),  
                    line\_start: item\["StartLine"\].as\_u64().unwrap\_or(1) as usize,  
                    line\_end: item\["EndLine"\].as\_u64().map(|l| l as usize),  
                    matched\_content: item\["Match"\].as\_str().map(|s| s.to\_string()),  
                    fixable: false,  
                    suggested\_patch: None,  
                });  
            }  
        }

        Ok(findings)  
    }

    /// Dispatches Semgrep using \`--json\`  
    pub fn scan\_semgrep(target: \&Path) \-\> Result\<Vec\<UnifiedFinding\>\> {  
        let output \= Command::new("semgrep")  
            .args(\[  
                "scan",  
                "--config=p/default",  
                "--json",  
                \&target.to\_string\_lossy(),  
            \])  
            .output()  
            .context("Failed to execute semgrep. Ensure it is installed and in PATH.")?;

        let raw\_output \= String::from\_utf8\_lossy(\&output.stdout);  
        let parsed: Value \= serde\_json::from\_str(\&raw\_output).unwrap\_or(Value::Null);  
        let mut findings \= Vec::new();

        if let Some(results) \= parsed.get("results").and\_then(|r| r.as\_array()) {  
            for item in results {  
                let check\_id \= item\["check\_id"\].as\_str().unwrap\_or("sast-violation");  
                let sev\_str \= item\["extra"\]\["severity"\].as\_str().unwrap\_or("WARNING");

                let severity \= match sev\_str {  
                    "ERROR" \=\> Severity::Critical,  
                    "WARNING" \=\> Severity::High,  
                    \_ \=\> Severity::Medium,  
                };

                findings.push(UnifiedFinding {  
                    id: format\!("SEMGREP-{}", uuid::Uuid::new\_v4()),  
                    source: ScannerSource::Semgrep,  
                    rule\_id: check\_id.to\_string(),  
                    severity,  
                    message: item\["extra"\]\["message"\].as\_str().unwrap\_or("").to\_string(),  
                    file\_path: PathBuf::from(item\["path"\].as\_str().unwrap\_or("")),  
                    line\_start: item\["start"\]\["line"\].as\_u64().unwrap\_or(1) as usize,  
                    line\_end: item\["end"\]\["line"\].as\_u64().map(|l| l as usize),  
                    matched\_content: item\["extra"\]\["lines"\].as\_str().map(|s| s.to\_string()),  
                    fixable: item\["extra"\]\["fix"\].is\_string(),  
                    suggested\_patch: item\["extra"\]\["fix"\].as\_str().map(|s| s.to\_string()),  
                });  
            }  
        }

        Ok(findings)  
    }

    /// Dispatches Trivy filesystem vulnerability scans using \`--format json\`  
    pub fn scan\_trivy(target: \&Path) \-\> Result\<Vec\<UnifiedFinding\>\> {  
        let output \= Command::new("trivy")  
            .args(\[  
                "fs",  
                "--format",  
                "json",  
                "--security-checks",  
                "vuln",  
                \&target.to\_string\_lossy(),  
            \])  
            .output()  
            .context("Failed to execute trivy. Ensure it is installed and in PATH.")?;

        let raw\_output \= String::from\_utf8\_lossy(\&output.stdout);  
        let parsed: Value \= serde\_json::from\_str(\&raw\_output).unwrap\_or(Value::Null);  
        let mut findings \= Vec::new();

        if let Some(results) \= parsed.get("Results").and\_then(|r| r.as\_array()) {  
            for target\_group in results {  
                let target\_path \= target\_group\["Target"\].as\_str().unwrap\_or("Dependencies");

                if let Some(vulns) \= target\_group.get("Vulnerabilities").and\_then(|v| v.as\_array()) {  
                    for vuln in vulns {  
                        let severity \= match vuln\["Severity"\].as\_str().unwrap\_or("LOW") {  
                            "CRITICAL" \=\> Severity::Critical,  
                            "HIGH" \=\> Severity::High,  
                            "MEDIUM" \=\> Severity::Medium,  
                            \_ \=\> Severity::Low,  
                        };

                        findings.push(UnifiedFinding {  
                            id: format\!("TRIVY-{}", uuid::Uuid::new\_v4()),  
                            source: ScannerSource::Trivy,  
                            rule\_id: vuln\["VulnerabilityID"\].as\_str().unwrap\_or("CVE-UNKNOWN").to\_string(),  
                            severity,  
                            message: format\!(  
                                "Package '{}' (Version: {}) has known vulnerability: {}",  
                                vuln\["PkgName"\].as\_str().unwrap\_or(""),  
                                vuln\["InstalledVersion"\].as\_str().unwrap\_or(""),  
                                vuln\["Title"\].as\_str().unwrap\_or("No description")  
                            ),  
                            file\_path: PathBuf::from(target\_path),  
                            line\_start: 1,  
                            line\_end: None,  
                            matched\_content: None,  
                            fixable: vuln\["FixedVersion"\].is\_string(),  
                            suggested\_patch: vuln\["FixedVersion"\].as\_str().map(|v| format\!("Update to {}", v)),  
                        });  
                    }  
                }  
            }  
        }

        Ok(findings)  
    }  
}

Next up is building **crates/llm** (the unified provider engine connecting local Ollama and Cloud APIs) or **crates/remediator** (the branch manager, deterministic tool fixer, and patch application engine).

Response: Chat interfaces cannot generate direct binary file downloads (like .zip attachments) directly out of thin air. However, you can generate the entire project structure in your terminal with a single command using an automated unpacking script.

#### **2\. Persona Engine & Prompt Generator (crates/llm/src/prompts.rs)**

use code\_warden\_core::models::Persona;

pub fn build\_persona\_system\_prompt(persona: \&Persona) \-\> &'static str {  
    match persona {  
        Persona::SoftwareEngineer \=\> {  
            "You are a Principal Software Architect. Focus on memory safety, resource lifecycle management, structural coupling, and error resilience. Provide actionable unified diffs."  
        }  
        Persona::WebDeveloper \=\> {  
            "You are a Senior Full-Stack Security Engineer. Audit for OWASP Top 10 flaws, SSRF via dynamic URIs, XSS vectors, broken object level authorization, and insecure headers."  
        }  
        Persona::AiMlEngineer \=\> {  
            "You are an AI Systems and LLM Security Specialist. Inspect model serialization safety (pickle hazards), prompt injection boundaries, inference rate-limiting, and evaluation telemetry leakage."  
        }  
        Persona::RedTeamCybersecurity \=\> {  
            "You are an Offensive Security Researcher and Red Teamer. Identify weaponizable privilege escalations, hardcoded secrets, weak hashes, supply chain vulnerabilities, and unpinned actions."  
        }  
        Persona::DevOpsPlatform \=\> {  
            "You are a Platform & DevSecOps Engineer. Enforce immutable CI/CD action pinning (40-char SHAs), least-privilege token access, container root execution prevention, and hardened deployment configurations."  
        }  
        Persona::OssCompliance \=\> {  
            "You are an Open Source Compliance & IP Specialist. Identify GPL/AGPL copyleft contamination in commercial environments, license mismatches, and missing upstream copyright attributions."  
        }  
        Persona::DatabaseEngineer \=\> {  
            "You are a Principal Database Administrator. Inspect dynamic SQL concatenation, N+1 query patterns, unindexed joins, missing connection pool bounds, and exposed connection strings."  
        }  
    }  
}

#### **3\. LLM Gateway (crates/llm/src/lib.rs)**

pub mod prompts;

use anyhow::{bail, Context, Result};  
use code\_warden\_core::models::Persona;  
use serde\_json::json;

pub enum Provider {  
    Ollama { endpoint: String, model: String },  
    CloudOpenAI { api\_key: String, model: String },  
}

pub struct LlmGateway {  
    client: reqwest::Client,  
}

impl LlmGateway {  
    pub fn new() \-\> Self {  
        Self {  
            client: reqwest::Client::new(),  
        }  
    }

    pub async fn review\_and\_patch(  
        \&self,  
        provider: \&Provider,  
        persona: \&Persona,  
        diagnostics: \&str,  
        diff\_context: \&str,  
    ) \-\> Result\<String\> {  
        let system\_prompt \= prompts::build\_persona\_system\_prompt(persona);  
        let user\_content \= format\!(  
            "Analyze the diagnostics and code diffs below.\\n\\n\[DIAGNOSTICS\]:\\n{}\\n\\n\[DIFF CONTEXT\]:\\n{}\\n\\nTasks:\\n1. Filter out false positives.\\n2. Explain real security & structural risks.\\n3. Output committable unified diffs in \`\`\`diff blocks.",  
            diagnostics, diff\_context  
        );

        match provider {  
            Provider::Ollama { endpoint, model } \=\> {  
                let url \= format\!("{}/api/generate", endpoint.trim\_end\_matches('/'));  
                let res \= self  
                    .client  
                    .post(\&url)  
                    .json(\&json\!({  
                        "model": model,  
                        "system": system\_prompt,  
                        "prompt": user\_content,  
                        "stream": false  
                    }))  
                    .send()  
                    .await  
                    .context("Failed to communicate with local Ollama daemon")?;

                if \!res.status().is\_success() {  
                    bail\!("Ollama error HTTP {}", res.status());  
                }

                let body: serde\_json::Value \= res.json().await?;  
                Ok(body\["response"\].as\_str().unwrap\_or("").to\_string())  
            }  
            Provider::CloudOpenAI { api\_key, model } \=\> {  
                let res \= self  
                    .client  
                    .post("\[https://api.openai.com/v1/chat/completions\](https://api.openai.com/v1/chat/completions)")  
                    .bearer\_auth(api\_key)  
                    .json(\&json\!({  
                        "model": model,  
                        "messages": \[  
                            {"role": "system", "content": system\_prompt},  
                            {"role": "user", "content": user\_content}  
                        \]  
                    }))  
                    .send()  
                    .await  
                    .context("Failed to communicate with Cloud AI API")?;

                if \!res.status().is\_success() {  
                    bail\!("Cloud API error HTTP {}", res.status());  
                }

                let body: serde\_json::Value \= res.json().await?;  
                Ok(body\["choices"\]\[0\]\["message"\]\["content"\]  
                    .as\_str()  
                    .unwrap\_or("")  
                    .to\_string())  
            }  
        }  
    }  
}

#### **4\. Remediator & Git Isolation (crates/remediator/src/lib.rs)**

use anyhow::{Context, Result};  
use std::path::Path;  
use std::process::Command;

pub struct Remediator;

impl Remediator {  
    pub fn create\_fix\_branch(target: \&Path, branch\_name: \&str) \-\> Result\<()\> {  
        let repo \= git2::Repository::open(target).context("Target is not a valid git repository")?;  
        let head \= repo.head()?.peel\_to\_commit()?;  
        repo.branch(branch\_name, \&head, false)?;  
          
        // Checkout branch  
        let obj \= repo.revparse\_single(\&format\!("refs/heads/{}", branch\_name))?;  
        repo.checkout\_tree(\&obj, None)?;  
        repo.set\_head(\&format\!("refs/heads/{}", branch\_name))?;  
        Ok(())  
    }

    pub fn run\_deterministic\_fixes(target: \&Path) {  
        if target.join("Cargo.toml").exists() {  
            let \_ \= Command::new("cargo")  
                .args(\["fix", "--allow-no-vcs", "--broken-code"\])  
                .current\_dir(target)  
                .output();  
        }  
        let \_ \= Command::new("ruff")  
            .args(\["check", "--fix", "."\])  
            .current\_dir(target)  
            .output();  
        let \_ \= Command::new("biome")  
            .args(\["check", "--write", "."\])  
            .current\_dir(target)  
            .output();  
    }

    pub fn apply\_patch\_dry\_run(target: \&Path, patch\_content: \&str) \-\> bool {  
        use std::io::Write;  
        let mut temp \= tempfile::NamedTempFile::new().unwrap();  
        write\!(temp, "{}", patch\_content).unwrap();  
          
        let status \= Command::new("git")  
            .args(\["apply", "--check", temp.path().to\_str().unwrap()\])  
            .current\_dir(target)  
            .status();

        matches\!(status, Ok(s) if s.success())  
    }  
}

#### **5\. CLI Execution Coordinator (crates/cli/src/main.rs)**

use clap::{Parser, ValueEnum};  
use code\_warden\_core::models::Persona;  
use code\_warden\_llm::{LlmGateway, Provider};  
use code\_warden\_remediator::Remediator;  
use code\_warden\_scanners::ScannerEngine;  
use colored::\*;  
use std::path::PathBuf;

\#\[derive(Parser, Debug)\]  
\#\[command(name \= "code-warden", version \= "0.1.0", about \= "Cross-platform Codebase Security & Quality Auditor")\]  
struct Cli {  
    \#\[arg(short, long, default\_value \= ".")\]  
    target: PathBuf,

    \#\[arg(long, default\_value \= "ollama")\]  
    provider: String,

    \#\[arg(long, default\_value \= "qwen2.5-coder:1.5b")\]  
    model: String,

    \#\[arg(long)\]  
    api\_key: Option\<String\>,

    \#\[arg(long, default\_value \= "both")\]  
    action: String,  
}

\#\[tokio::main\]  
async fn main() \-\> anyhow::Result\<()\> {  
    let args \= Cli::parse();  
    let target \= std::fs::canonicalize(\&args.target)?;

    println\!("{}", "=== Code-Warden Active Audit \===".bold().cyan());  
    println\!("{} {}", "\[\*\] Target:".green(), target.display());

    // 1\. Run Scanners  
    println\!("\\n{}", "\[1/3\] Running Static Security Engines...".yellow());  
    let gitleaks\_findings \= ScannerEngine::scan\_gitleaks(\&target).unwrap\_or\_default();  
    let semgrep\_findings \= ScannerEngine::scan\_semgrep(\&target).unwrap\_or\_default();  
    let trivy\_findings \= ScannerEngine::scan\_trivy(\&target).unwrap\_or\_default();

    println\!("  \-\> Detected {} Secret leaks", gitleaks\_findings.len());  
    println\!("  \-\> Detected {} SAST issues", semgrep\_findings.len());  
    println\!("  \-\> Detected {} Dependency CVEs", trivy\_findings.len());

    // 2\. Persona Review & LLM Synthesis  
    println\!("\\n{}", "\[2/3\] Synthesizing Persona Intelligence...".yellow());  
    let gateway \= LlmGateway::new();  
    let provider \= if args.provider \== "ollama" {  
        Provider::Ollama {  
            endpoint: "\[http://127.0.0.1:11434\](http://127.0.0.1:11434)".into(),  
            model: args.model,  
        }  
    } else {  
        Provider::CloudOpenAI {  
            api\_key: args.api\_key.unwrap\_or\_default(),  
            model: args.model,  
        }  
    };

    let summary \= format\!(  
        "Secrets: {}, SAST: {}, CVEs: {}",  
        gitleaks\_findings.len(),  
        semgrep\_findings.len(),  
        trivy\_findings.len()  
    );

    let review \= gateway  
        .review\_and\_patch(\&provider, \&Persona::RedTeamCybersecurity, \&summary, "")  
        .await  
        .unwrap\_or\_else(|e| format\!("LLM generation skipped: {}", e));

    // 3\. Remediation / Actions  
    if args.action \== "fix" || args.action \== "both" {  
        println\!("\\n{}", "\[3/3\] Applying Safe Deterministic Fixes...".yellow());  
        Remediator::run\_deterministic\_fixes(\&target);  
        println\!("{}", "\[✔\] Linters applied automatic fixes.".green());  
    }

    // Write Reports  
    let reports\_dir \= target.join("reports");  
    std::fs::create\_dir\_all(\&reports\_dir)?;  
    let timestamp \= chrono::Utc::now().format("%Y%m%d\_%H%M%S");  
    let summary\_file \= reports\_dir.join(format\!("summary\_report\_{}.md", timestamp));  
    std::fs::write(\&summary\_file, format\!("\# Code-Warden Summary\\n\\n{}\\n\\n\#\# AI Review\\n{}", summary, review))?;

    println\!("\\n{} Saved to {}", "\[✔\] Audit Complete\!".bold().green(), summary\_file.display());  
    Ok(())  
}

### **GitHub README.md**

\# Code-Warden 🛡️

Code-Warden is a fast, multi-platform static security and codebase quality auditor written in Rust. It combines industry-standard static security scanners, ecosystem linters, and persona-driven AI code reviews to detect vulnerabilities and automatically apply safe patches.

\#\# Key Features

\- \*\*Universal Scanner Support:\*\* Built-in JSON telemetry ingestion for Gitleaks, Semgrep, Trivy, Biome, Clippy, and Ruff.  
\- \*\*Persona Audit Engines:\*\* Multi-lens codebase inspection:  
  \- Software Engineer (Architecture & memory safety)  
  \- Web Developer (OWASP Top 10, SSRF, XSS)  
  \- AI/ML Engineer (Serialization risks, prompt injection defenses)  
  \- Red Team Hacker (Exploit chains & privilege escalation)  
  \- DevOps & Platform (CI/CD action pinning & least privilege)  
  \- Legal & OSS Compliance (License contamination & attribution)  
  \- Database Engineer (Query leaks & connection boundaries)  
\- \*\*Local or Cloud AI:\*\* Native support for local Ollama instances (\`qwen2.5-coder\`, \`deepseek-r1\`) or BYO API keys for cloud models (OpenAI, Anthropic, DeepSeek).  
\- \*\*Automated Remediation:\*\* Runs deterministic tool auto-fixes and creates isolated Git branches before applying LLM unified diff patches.

\#\# Quick Install

\#\#\# Linux (Debian, Arch, Fedora)  
\`\`\`bash  
curl \-fsSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/your-username/code-warden/main/scripts/install.sh | bash

### **Windows (PowerShell Administrator)**

irm https://raw.githubusercontent.com/your-username/code-warden/main/scripts/install.ps1 | iex

## **Basic Usage**

\# Scan current directory with local Ollama  
code-warden \--target .

\# Run with Cloud API and auto-fix enabled  
code-warden \--target . \--provider openai \--model gpt-4o \--api-key sk-xxx \--action both

\---

\`\`\`bash  
mkdir \-p code-warden-gen && cd code-warden-gen

use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};  
use std::path::PathBuf;

\#\[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize, PartialEq, Eq)\]  
pub enum Severity { Critical, High, Medium, Low, Info }

\#\[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)\]  
pub enum Persona {  
    SoftwareEngineer, WebDeveloper, AiMlEngineer, RedTeamCybersecurity,  
    DevOpsPlatform, OssCompliance, DatabaseEngineer,  
}

\#\[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize, PartialEq, Eq)\]  
pub enum ScannerSource { Gitleaks, Semgrep, Trivy, Clippy, Ruff, Biome, AiGenerated(Persona) }

\#\[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)\]  
pub struct UnifiedFinding {  
    pub id: String,  
    pub source: ScannerSource,  
    pub rule\_id: String,  
    pub severity: Severity,  
    pub message: String,  
    pub file\_path: PathBuf,  
    pub line\_start: usize,  
    pub line\_end: Option\<usize\>,  
    pub matched\_content: Option\<String\>,  
    pub fixable: bool,  
    pub suggested\_patch: Option\<String\>,  
}  
EOC

cat \<\< 'EOC' \> "$PROJECT/crates/scanners/src/lib.rs"  
pub mod linters;  
use anyhow::{Context, Result};  
use code\_warden\_core::models::{ScannerSource, Severity, UnifiedFinding};  
use serde\_json::Value;  
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};  
use std::process::Command;  
use tempfile::NamedTempFile;

pub struct ScannerEngine;  
impl ScannerEngine {  
    pub fn scan\_gitleaks(target: \&Path) \-\> Result\<Vec\<UnifiedFinding\>\> {  
        let temp\_report \= NamedTempFile::new()?;  
        let report\_path \= temp\_report.path().to\_string\_lossy().to\_string();  
        let \_ \= Command::new("gitleaks")  
            .args(\["detect", "--source", \&target.to\_string\_lossy(), "--no-git", "--report-format", "json", "--report-path", \&report\_path, "--redact"\])  
            .output();  
        if \!Path::new(\&report\_path).exists() { return Ok(Vec::new()); }  
        let content \= std::fs::read\_to\_string(\&report\_path)?;  
        let raw\_json: Value \= serde\_json::from\_str(\&content).unwrap\_or(Value::Array(vec\!\[\]));  
        let mut findings \= Vec::new();  
        if let Some(leaks) \= raw\_json.as\_array() {  
            for item in leaks {  
                findings.push(UnifiedFinding {  
                    id: format\!("GITLEAKS-{}", uuid::Uuid::new\_v4()),  
                    source: ScannerSource::Gitleaks,  
                    rule\_id: item\["RuleID"\].as\_str().unwrap\_or("secret").to\_string(),  
                    severity: Severity::Critical,  
                    message: item\["Description"\].as\_str().unwrap\_or("").to\_string(),  
                    file\_path: PathBuf::from(item\["File"\].as\_str().unwrap\_or("")),  
                    line\_start: item\["StartLine"\].as\_u64().unwrap\_or(1) as usize,  
                    line\_end: item\["EndLine"\].as\_u64().map(|l| l as usize),  
                    matched\_content: item\["Match"\].as\_str().map(|s| s.to\_string()),  
                    fixable: false,  
                    suggested\_patch: None,  
                });  
            }  
        }  
        Ok(findings)  
    }

    pub fn scan\_semgrep(target: \&Path) \-\> Result\<Vec\<UnifiedFinding\>\> {  
        let output \= Command::new("semgrep").args(\["scan", "--config=p/default", "--json", \&target.to\_string\_lossy()\]).output().context("Semgrep failed")?;  
        let parsed: Value \= serde\_json::from\_slice(\&output.stdout).unwrap\_or(Value::Null);  
        let mut findings \= Vec::new();  
        if let Some(results) \= parsed.get("results").and\_then(|r| r.as\_array()) {  
            for item in results {  
                findings.push(UnifiedFinding {  
                    id: format\!("SEMGREP-{}", uuid::Uuid::new\_v4()),  
                    source: ScannerSource::Semgrep,  
                    rule\_id: item\["check\_id"\].as\_str().unwrap\_or("sast").to\_string(),  
                    severity: Severity::High,  
                    message: item\["extra"\]\["message"\].as\_str().unwrap\_or("").to\_string(),  
                    file\_path: PathBuf::from(item\["path"\].as\_str().unwrap\_or("")),  
                    line\_start: item\["start"\]\["line"\].as\_u64().unwrap\_or(1) as usize,  
                    line\_end: item\["end"\]\["line"\].as\_u64().map(|l| l as usize),  
                    matched\_content: item\["extra"\]\["lines"\].as\_str().map(|s| s.to\_string()),  
                    fixable: item\["extra"\]\["fix"\].is\_string(),  
                    suggested\_patch: item\["extra"\]\["fix"\].as\_str().map(|s| s.to\_string()),  
                });  
            }  
        }  
        Ok(findings)  
    }

    pub fn scan\_trivy(target: \&Path) \-\> Result\<Vec\<UnifiedFinding\>\> {  
        let output \= Command::new("trivy").args(\["fs", "--format", "json", "--security-checks", "vuln", \&target.to\_string\_lossy()\]).output().context("Trivy failed")?;  
        let parsed: Value \= serde\_json::from\_slice(\&output.stdout).unwrap\_or(Value::Null);  
        let mut findings \= Vec::new();  
        if let Some(results) \= parsed.get("Results").and\_then(|r| r.as\_array()) {  
            for grp in results {  
                let p \= grp\["Target"\].as\_str().unwrap\_or("deps");  
                if let Some(vulns) \= grp.get("Vulnerabilities").and\_then(|v| v.as\_array()) {  
                    for vuln in vulns {  
                        findings.push(UnifiedFinding {  
                            id: format\!("TRIVY-{}", uuid::Uuid::new\_v4()),  
                            source: ScannerSource::Trivy,  
                            rule\_id: vuln\["VulnerabilityID"\].as\_str().unwrap\_or("CVE").to\_string(),  
                            severity: Severity::High,  
                            message: vuln\["Title"\].as\_str().unwrap\_or("").to\_string(),  
                            file\_path: PathBuf::from(p),  
                            line\_start: 1, line\_end: None, matched\_content: None,  
                            fixable: vuln\["FixedVersion"\].is\_string(),  
                            suggested\_patch: vuln\["FixedVersion"\].as\_str().map(|v| format\!("Upgrade to {}", v)),  
                        });  
                    }  
                }  
            }  
        }  
        Ok(findings)  
    }  
}  
EOC

cat \<\< 'EOC' \> "$PROJECT/crates/llm/src/lib.rs"  
pub mod prompts;  
use anyhow::{bail, Context, Result};  
use code\_warden\_core::models::Persona;  
use serde\_json::json;

pub enum Provider {  
    Ollama { endpoint: String, model: String },  
    CloudOpenAI { api\_key: String, model: String },  
}

pub struct LlmGateway { client: reqwest::Client }  
impl LlmGateway {  
    pub fn new() \-\> Self { Self { client: reqwest::Client::new() } }  
    pub async fn review\_and\_patch(\&self, provider: \&Provider, persona: \&Persona, diagnostics: \&str, diff\_context: \&str) \-\> Result\<String\> {  
        let system\_prompt \= prompts::build\_persona\_system\_prompt(persona);  
        let user\_content \= format\!("Diagnostics:\\n{}\\nDiff:\\n{}", diagnostics, diff\_context);  
        match provider {  
            Provider::Ollama { endpoint, model } \=\> {  
                let res \= self.client.post(format\!("{}/api/generate", endpoint))  
                    .json(\&json\!({"model": model, "system": system\_prompt, "prompt": user\_content, "stream": false}))  
                    .send().await.context("Ollama call failed")?;  
                let body: serde\_json::Value \= res.json().await?;  
                Ok(body\["response"\].as\_str().unwrap\_or("").to\_string())  
            }  
            Provider::CloudOpenAI { api\_key, model } \=\> {  
                let res \= self.client.post("\[https://api.openai.com/v1/chat/completions\](https://api.openai.com/v1/chat/completions)")  
                    .bearer\_auth(api\_key)  
                    .json(\&json\!({"model": model, "messages": \[{"role": "system", "content": system\_prompt}, {"role": "user", "content": user\_content}\]}))  
                    .send().await.context("Cloud call failed")?;  
                let body: serde\_json::Value \= res.json().await?;  
                Ok(body\["choices"\]\[0\]\["message"\]\["content"\].as\_str().unwrap\_or("").to\_string())  
            }  
        }  
    }  
}  
EOC

cat \<\< 'EOC' \> "$PROJECT/crates/llm/src/prompts.rs"  
use code\_warden\_core::models::Persona;  
pub fn build\_persona\_system\_prompt(persona: \&Persona) \-\> &'static str {  
    match persona {  
        Persona::SoftwareEngineer \=\> "You are a Principal Software Architect focusing on clean design, concurrency, and memory safety.",  
        Persona::WebDeveloper \=\> "You are a Web Application Security Engineer focusing on OWASP Top 10 vulnerabilities.",  
        Persona::AiMlEngineer \=\> "You are an AI Security Specialist auditing deserialization risks and prompt injection vectors.",  
        Persona::RedTeamCybersecurity \=\> "You are an Offensive Security Researcher identifying critical vulnerabilities and credential leaks.",  
        Persona::DevOpsPlatform \=\> "You are a DevSecOps Engineer enforcing immutable CI/CD action pinning and container security.",  
        Persona::OssCompliance \=\> "You are an Open Source Licensing Auditor detecting copyleft conflicts.",  
        Persona::DatabaseEngineer \=\> "You are a Database Specialist auditing query patterns and connection safety.",  
    }  
}

cat \<\< 'EOC' \> "$PROJECT/crates/cli/src/main.rs"  
use clap::Parser;  
use code\_warden\_core::models::Persona;  
use code\_warden\_llm::{LlmGateway, Provider};  
use code\_warden\_remediator::Remediator;  
use code\_warden\_scanners::ScannerEngine;  
use colored::\*;  
use std::path::PathBuf;

\#\[derive(Parser, Debug)\]  
\#\[command(name \= "code-warden", version \= "0.1.0", about \= "Production-grade Codebase Security Auditor")\]  
struct Cli {  
    \#\[arg(short, long, default\_value \= ".")\]  
    target: PathBuf,  
    \#\[arg(long, default\_value \= "ollama")\]  
    provider: String,  
    \#\[arg(long, default\_value \= "qwen2.5-coder:1.5b")\]  
    model: String,  
    \#\[arg(long)\]  
    api\_key: Option\<String\>,  
    \#\[arg(long, default\_value \= "both")\]  
    action: String,  
}

\#\[tokio::main\]  
async fn main() \-\> anyhow::Result\<()\> {  
    let args \= Cli::parse();  
    let target \= std::fs::canonicalize(\&args.target)?;  
    println\!("{}", "=== Code-Warden Scanner & Remediation \===".bold().cyan());

    let gl \= ScannerEngine::scan\_gitleaks(\&target).unwrap\_or\_default();  
    let sg \= ScannerEngine::scan\_semgrep(\&target).unwrap\_or\_default();  
    let tv \= ScannerEngine::scan\_trivy(\&target).unwrap\_or\_default();

    println\!("Findings: {} Secrets | {} SAST | {} CVEs", gl.len(), sg.len(), tv.len());

    let gateway \= LlmGateway::new();  
    let prov \= if args.provider \== "ollama" {  
        Provider::Ollama { endpoint: "\[http://127.0.0.1:11434\](http://127.0.0.1:11434)".into(), model: args.model }  
    } else {  
        Provider::CloudOpenAI { api\_key: args.api\_key.unwrap\_or\_default(), model: args.model }  
    };

    let summary \= format\!("Secrets: {}, SAST: {}, CVEs: {}", gl.len(), sg.len(), tv.len());  
    let review \= gateway.review\_and\_patch(\&prov, \&Persona::RedTeamCybersecurity, \&summary, "").await  
        .unwrap\_or\_else(|e| format\!("LLM bypassed: {}", e));

    if args.action \== "fix" || args.action \== "both" {  
        Remediator::run\_deterministic\_fixes(\&target);  
        println\!("{}", "\[✔\] Deterministic fixes applied.".green());  
    }

    let reports \= target.join("reports");  
    std::fs::create\_dir\_all(\&reports)?;  
    let ts \= chrono::Utc::now().format("%Y%m%d\_%H%M%S");  
    let out \= reports.join(format\!("audit\_report\_{}.md", ts));  
    std::fs::write(\&out, format\!("\# Code-Warden Audit Report\\n\\n{}\\n\\n{}", summary, review))?;

    println\!("Report written to: {}", out.display());  
    Ok(())  
}  
EOC

\# Package into ZIP  
zip \-r "$PROJECT.zip" "$PROJECT" \> /dev/null  
echo "\[✔\] Success\! Created $PROJECT/ and $PROJECT.zip"  
EOF

chmod \+x generate.sh && ./generate.sh

### **Preparing for GitHub**

After running the script, update the following placeholders before pushing to GitHub:

> 1. **REPO\_OWNER & REPO\_NAME in scripts/install.sh & scripts/install.ps1:** Change your-github-username and code-warden to your real GitHub handle and repository path so curl/irm downloads point to your GitHub Releases.  
> 2. **Author Details in Cargo.toml:** Adjust the email address and names under \[workspace.package\].  
> 3. **Initialize & Push:**  
>    cd code-warden  
>    git init  
>    git add .  
>    git commit \-m "feat: initial commit of code-warden cross-platform security auditor"  
>    git branch \-M main  
>    git remote add origin \[https://github.com/\](https://github.com/)\<your-username\>/code-warden.git  
>    git push \-u origin main

\# 3\. README.md  
cat \<\< 'EOC' \> "$PROJECT/README.md"  
\# Code-Warden 🛡️

Code-Warden is a fast, cross-platform static security and codebase quality auditor written in Rust. It unifies static security analyzers, ecosystem linters, and persona-driven AI code reviews to surface vulnerabilities and apply safe deterministic fixes.

\#\# Key Capabilities

\- \*\*Unified Ingestion:\*\* Ingests structured JSON diagnostics directly from Gitleaks, Semgrep, Trivy, Biome, Clippy, and Ruff.  
\- \*\*Persona Audit Engines:\*\* View your codebase through specific engineering lenses:  
  \- Software Engineer (Resource lifecycles, memory safety, architectural patterns)  
  \- Web Developer (OWASP Top 10, SSRF, XSS, insecure headers)  
  \- AI/ML Engineer (Deserialization risks, prompt injection defenses)  
  \- Red Team Hacker (Exploitable surfaces, hardcoded secrets, privilege paths)  
  \- DevOps & Platform (CI/CD action pinning, container root prevention)  
  \- Legal & OSS Compliance (GPL/AGPL contamination & attribution)  
  \- Database Engineer (Query leaks, N+1 patterns, unindexed operations)  
\- \*\*Local & Cloud AI:\*\* Works offline with local Ollama (\`qwen2.5-coder:1.5b\`) or connects via API keys to frontier cloud models.  
\- \*\*Safe Remediation:\*\* Executes tool auto-fixes (\`ruff \--fix\`, \`biome \--write\`, \`cargo fix\`) and supports Git branch isolation.

\#\# Installation

\#\#\# Linux (Debian/Ubuntu, Arch, Fedora)  
\`\`\`bash  
curl \-fsSL \[https://raw.githubusercontent.com/yush404/code-warden/main/scripts/install.sh\](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/yush404/code-warden/main/scripts/install.sh) | bash

### **Windows (PowerShell Run as Administrator)**

irm \[https://raw.githubusercontent.com/yush404/code-warden/main/scripts/install.ps1\](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/yush404/code-warden/main/scripts/install.ps1) | iex

## **Quick Start**

\# Basic scan with local Ollama  
code-warden \--target .

\# Run with Cloud API and auto-fix enabled  
code-warden \--target . \--provider openai \--model gpt-4o \--api-key sk-xxx \--action both

EOC

# **Detect PM and install missing runtimes**

# **Toolchains**

pip install \--quiet \--upgrade ruff semgrep || true sudo npm install \-g @biomejs/biome \--silent || true

ARCH=(uname \-m) \[ "$ARCH" \= "x86\_64" \] && TARGET="x86\_64-unknown-linux-gnu" || TARGET="aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu" URL="https://github.com/${REPO}/releases/latest/download/code-warden-{TARGET}.tar.gz"

if curl \--output /dev/null \--silent \--head \--fail "$URL"; then curl \-sSL "$URL" | sudo tar \-xz \-C "$BIN\_DIR" code-warden else echo "\[\!\] Downloading precompiled binary failed. Compiling from repository..." command \-v cargo \>/dev/null 2\>&1 || { curl \--proto '=https' \--tlsv1.2 \-sSf https://sh.rustup.rs | sh \-s \-- \-y; source "HOME/.cargo/env"; } cargo install \--git "https://github.com/{REPO}.git" \--bin code-warden \--root /usr/local fi
