cat \<\< 'EOC' \> "$PROJECT/scripts/install.ps1" Write-Host "=== Installing Code-Warden for Windows \===" \-ForegroundColor Cyan $InstallDir \= "$env:ProgramFiles\\Code-Warden" if (-not (Test-Path \-Path $InstallDir)) { New-Item \-ItemType Directory \-Path $InstallDir | Out-Null }

winget install \--id Git.Git \-e \--silent \--accept-source-agreements \--accept-package-agreements winget install \--id Python.Python.3.12 \-e \--silent \--accept-source-agreements \--accept-package-agreements winget install \--id OpenJS.NodeJS.LTS \-e \--silent \--accept-source-agreements \--accept-package-agreements winget install \--id Ollama.Ollama \-e \--silent \--accept-source-agreements \--accept-package-agreements winget install \--id Gitleaks.Gitleaks \-e \--silent \--accept-source-agreements \--accept-package-agreements winget install \--id AquaSecurity.Trivy \-e \--silent \--accept-source-agreements \--accept-package-agreements pip install \--upgrade ruff semgrep | Out-Null npm install \-g @biomejs/biome \--silent | Out-Null

$Repo \= "yush404/code-warden" $AssetUrl \= "https://github.com/$Repo/releases/latest/download/code-warden-x86\_64-pc-windows-msvc.zip" $ZipPath \= "$env:TEMP\\code-warden.zip" Invoke-WebRequest \-Uri $AssetUrl \-OutFile $ZipPath Expand-Archive \-Path $ZipPath \-DestinationPath $InstallDir \-Force Remove-Item \-Path $ZipPath

$CurrentPath \= \[Environment\]::GetEnvironmentVariable("Path", \[EnvironmentVariableTarget\]::Machine) if ($CurrentPath \-notlike "*$InstallDir*") { \[Environment\]::SetEnvironmentVariable("Path", "$CurrentPath;$InstallDir", \[EnvironmentVariableTarget\]::Machine) } Write-Host "\[✔\] Code-Warden installed to $InstallDir" \-ForegroundColor Green EOC

# **5\. crates/core**

cat \<\< 'EOC' \> "$PROJECT/crates/core/Cargo.toml" \[package\] name \= "code-warden-core" version.workspace \= true edition.workspace \= true \[dependencies\] serde.workspace \= true chrono.workspace \= true EOC

cat \<\< 'EOC' \> "$PROJECT/crates/core/src/lib.rs" pub mod models; EOC

cat \<\< 'EOC' \> "$PROJECT/crates/core/src/models.rs" use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize}; use std::path::PathBuf;

\#\[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize, PartialEq, Eq)\] pub enum Severity { Critical, High, Medium, Low, Info }

\#\[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)\] pub enum Persona { SoftwareEngineer, WebDeveloper, AiMlEngineer, RedTeamCybersecurity, DevOpsPlatform, OssCompliance, DatabaseEngineer, }

\#\[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize, PartialEq, Eq)\] pub enum ScannerSource { Gitleaks, Semgrep, Trivy, Clippy, Ruff, Biome, AiGenerated(Persona) }

\#\[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)\] pub struct UnifiedFinding { pub id: String, pub source: ScannerSource, pub rule\_id: String, pub severity: Severity, pub message: String, pub file\_path: PathBuf, pub line\_start: usize, pub line\_end: Option, pub matched\_content: Option, pub fixable: bool, pub suggested\_patch: Option, } EOC

# **6\. crates/scanners**

cat \<\< 'EOC' \> "$PROJECT/crates/scanners/Cargo.toml" \[package\] name \= "code-warden-scanners" version.workspace \= true edition.workspace \= true \[dependencies\] code-warden-core \= { path \= "../core" } anyhow.workspace \= true serde\_json.workspace \= true uuid.workspace \= true tempfile.workspace \= true EOC

cat \<\< 'EOC' \> "$PROJECT/crates/scanners/src/lib.rs" use anyhow::{Context, Result}; use code\_warden\_core::models::{ScannerSource, Severity, UnifiedFinding}; use serde\_json::Value; use std::path::{Path, PathBuf}; use std::process::Command; use tempfile::NamedTempFile;

pub struct ScannerEngine; impl ScannerEngine { pub fn scan\_gitleaks(target: \&Path) \-\> Result\<Vec\> { let temp\_report \= NamedTempFile::new()?; let report\_path \= temp\_report.path().to\_string\_lossy().to\_string(); let \_ \= Command::new("gitleaks") .args(\["detect", "--source", \&target.to\_string\_lossy(), "--no-git", "--report-format", "json", "--report-path", \&report\_path, "--redact"\]) .output(); if \!Path::new(\&report\_path).exists() { return Ok(Vec::new()); } let content \= std::fs::read\_to\_string(\&report\_path)?; let raw\_json: Value \= serde\_json::from\_str(\&content).unwrap\_or(Value::Array(vec\!\[\])); let mut findings \= Vec::new(); if let Some(leaks) \= raw\_json.as\_array() { for item in leaks { findings.push(UnifiedFinding { id: format\!("GITLEAKS-{}", uuid::Uuid::new\_v4()), source: ScannerSource::Gitleaks, rule\_id: item\["RuleID"\].as\_str().unwrap\_or("secret").to\_string(), severity: Severity::Critical, message: item\["Description"\].as\_str().unwrap\_or("").to\_string(), file\_path: PathBuf::from(item\["File"\].as\_str().unwrap\_or("")), line\_start: item\["StartLine"\].as\_u64().unwrap\_or(1) as usize, line\_end: item\["EndLine"\].as\_u64().map(|l| l as usize), matched\_content: item\["Match"\].as\_str().map(|s| s.to\_string()), fixable: false, suggested\_patch: None, }); } } Ok(findings) }

pub fn scan\_semgrep(target: \&Path) \-\> Result\<Vec\<UnifiedFinding\>\> {  
    let output \= Command::new("semgrep").args(\["scan", "--config=p/default", "--json", \&target.to\_string\_lossy()\]).output().context("Semgrep failed")?;  
    let parsed: Value \= serde\_json::from\_slice(\&output.stdout).unwrap\_or(Value::Null);  
    let mut findings \= Vec::new();  
    if let Some(results) \= parsed.get("results").and\_then(|r| r.as\_array()) {  
        for item in results {  
            findings.push(UnifiedFinding {  
                id: format\!("SEMGREP-{}", uuid::Uuid::new\_v4()),  
                source: ScannerSource::Semgrep,  
                rule\_id: item\["check\_id"\].as\_str().unwrap\_or("sast").to\_string(),  
                severity: Severity::High,  
                message: item\["extra"\]\["message"\].as\_str().unwrap\_or("").to\_string(),  
                file\_path: PathBuf::from(item\["path"\].as\_str().unwrap\_or("")),  
                line\_start: item\["start"\]\["line"\].as\_u64().unwrap\_or(1) as usize,  
                line\_end: item\["end"\]\["line"\].as\_u64().map(|l| l as usize),  
                matched\_content: item\["extra"\]\["lines"\].as\_str().map(|s| s.to\_string()),  
                fixable: item\["extra"\]\["fix"\].is\_string(),  
                suggested\_patch: item\["extra"\]\["fix"\].as\_str().map(|s| s.to\_string()),  
            });  
        }  
    }  
    Ok(findings)  
}

pub fn scan\_trivy(target: \&Path) \-\> Result\<Vec\<UnifiedFinding\>\> {  
    let output \= Command::new("trivy").args(\["fs", "--format", "json", "--security-checks", "vuln", \&target.to\_string\_lossy()\]).output().context("Trivy failed")?;  
    let parsed: Value \= serde\_json::from\_slice(\&output.stdout).unwrap\_or(Value::Null);  
    let mut findings \= Vec::new();  
    if let Some(results) \= parsed.get("Results").and\_then(|r| r.as\_array()) {  
        for grp in results {  
            let p \= grp\["Target"\].as\_str().unwrap\_or("deps");  
            if let Some(vulns) \= grp.get("Vulnerabilities").and\_then(|v| v.as\_array()) {  
                for vuln in vulns {  
                    findings.push(UnifiedFinding {  
                        id: format\!("TRIVY-{}", uuid::Uuid::new\_v4()),  
                        source: ScannerSource::Trivy,  
                        rule\_id: vuln\["VulnerabilityID"\].as\_str().unwrap\_or("CVE").to\_string(),  
                        severity: Severity::High,  
                        message: vuln\["Title"\].as\_str().unwrap\_or("").to\_string(),  
                        file\_path: PathBuf::from(p),  
                        line\_start: 1, line\_end: None, matched\_content: None,  
                        fixable: vuln\["FixedVersion"\].is\_string(),  
                        suggested\_patch: vuln\["FixedVersion"\].as\_str().map(|v| format\!("Upgrade to {}", v)),  
                    });  
                }  
            }  
        }  
    }  
    Ok(findings)  
}

} EOC

# **7\. crates/llm**

cat \<\< 'EOC' \> "$PROJECT/crates/llm/Cargo.toml" \[package\] name \= "code-warden-llm" version.workspace \= true edition.workspace \= true \[dependencies\] code-warden-core \= { path \= "../core" } anyhow.workspace \= true reqwest.workspace \= true serde\_json.workspace \= true EOC

cat \<\< 'EOC' \> "$PROJECT/crates/llm/src/lib.rs" pub mod prompts; use anyhow::{bail, Context, Result}; use code\_warden\_core::models::Persona; use serde\_json::json;

pub enum Provider { Ollama { endpoint: String, model: String }, CloudOpenAI { api\_key: String, model: String }, }

pub struct LlmGateway { client: reqwest::Client } impl LlmGateway { pub fn new() \-\> Self { Self { client: reqwest::Client::new() } } pub async fn review\_and\_patch(\&self, provider: \&Provider, persona: \&Persona, diagnostics: \&str, diff\_context: \&str) \-\> Result { let system\_prompt \= prompts::build\_persona\_system\_prompt(persona); let user\_content \= format\!("Diagnostics:\\n{}\\nDiff:\\n{}", diagnostics, diff\_context); match provider { Provider::Ollama { endpoint, model } \=\> { let res \= self.client.post(format\!("{}/api/generate", endpoint)) .json(\&json\!({"model": model, "system": system\_prompt, "prompt": user\_content, "stream": false})) .send().await.context("Ollama call failed")?; let body: serde\_json::Value \= res.json().await?; Ok(body\["response"\].as\_str().unwrap\_or("").to\_string()) } Provider::CloudOpenAI { api\_key, model } \=\> { let res \= self.client.post("https://api.openai.com/v1/chat/completions") .bearer\_auth(api\_key) .json(\&json\!({"model": model, "messages": \[{"role": "system", "content": system\_prompt}, {"role": "user", "content": user\_content}\]})) .send().await.context("Cloud call failed")?; let body: serde\_json::Value \= res.json().await?; Ok(body\["choices"\]\[0\]\["message"\]\["content"\].as\_str().unwrap\_or("").to\_string()) } } } } EOC

cat \<\< 'EOC' \> "$PROJECT/crates/llm/src/prompts.rs" use code\_warden\_core::models::Persona; pub fn build\_persona\_system\_prompt(persona: \&Persona) \-\> &'static str { match persona { Persona::SoftwareEngineer \=\> "You are a Principal Software Architect focusing on clean design, concurrency, and memory safety.", Persona::WebDeveloper \=\> "You are a Web Application Security Engineer focusing on OWASP Top 10 vulnerabilities.", Persona::AiMlEngineer \=\> "You are an AI Security Specialist auditing deserialization risks and prompt injection vectors.", Persona::RedTeamCybersecurity \=\> "You are an Offensive Security Researcher identifying critical vulnerabilities and credential leaks.", Persona::DevOpsPlatform \=\> "You are a DevSecOps Engineer enforcing immutable CI/CD action pinning and container security.", Persona::OssCompliance \=\> "You are an Open Source Licensing Auditor detecting copyleft conflicts.", Persona::DatabaseEngineer \=\> "You are a Database Specialist auditing query patterns and connection safety.", } } EOC

# **8\. crates/remediator**

cat \<\< 'EOC' \> "$PROJECT/crates/remediator/Cargo.toml" \[package\] name \= "code-warden-remediator" version.workspace \= true edition.workspace \= true \[dependencies\] anyhow.workspace \= true git2.workspace \= true tempfile.workspace \= true EOC

cat \<\< 'EOC' \> "$PROJECT/crates/remediator/src/lib.rs" pub mod git\_ops; use std::path::Path; use std::process::Command;

pub struct Remediator; impl Remediator { pub fn run\_deterministic\_fixes(target: \&Path) { if target.join("Cargo.toml").exists() { let \_ \= Command::new("cargo").args(\["fix", "--allow-no-vcs", "--broken-code"\]).current\_dir(target).output(); } let \_ \= Command::new("ruff").args(\["check", "--fix", "."\]).current\_dir(target).output(); let \_ \= Command::new("biome").args(\["check", "--write", "."\]).current\_dir(target).output(); } } EOC

cat \<\< 'EOC' \> "$PROJECT/crates/remediator/src/git\_ops.rs" use anyhow::{Context, Result}; use std::path::Path; pub fn create\_fix\_branch(target: \&Path, branch\_name: \&str) \-\> Result\<()\> { let repo \= git2::Repository::open(target).context("Failed to open repo")?; let head \= repo.head()?.peel\_to\_commit()?; repo.branch(branch\_name, \&head, false)?; let obj \= repo.revparse\_single(\&format\!("refs/heads/{}", branch\_name))?; repo.checkout\_tree(\&obj, None)?; repo.set\_head(\&format\!("refs/heads/{}", branch\_name))?; Ok(()) } EOC

# **9\. crates/cli**

cat \<\< 'EOC' \> "$PROJECT/crates/cli/Cargo.toml" \[package\] name \= "code-warden" version.workspace \= true edition.workspace \= true \[dependencies\] code-warden-core \= { path \= "../core" } code-warden-scanners \= { path \= "../scanners" } code-warden-llm \= { path \= "../llm" } code-warden-remediator \= { path \= "../remediator" } clap.workspace \= true tokio.workspace \= true anyhow.workspace \= true colored.workspace \= true chrono.workspace \= true EOC

cat \<\< 'EOC' \> "$PROJECT/crates/cli/src/main.rs" use clap::Parser; use code\_warden\_core::models::Persona; use code\_warden\_llm::{LlmGateway, Provider}; use code\_warden\_remediator::Remediator; use code\_warden\_scanners::ScannerEngine; use colored::\*; use std::path::PathBuf;

\#\[derive(Parser, Debug)\] \#\[command(name \= "code-warden", version \= "0.1.0", about \= "Production-grade Codebase Security Auditor")\] struct Cli { \#\[arg(short, long, default\_value \= ".")\] target: PathBuf, \#\[arg(long, default\_value \= "ollama")\] provider: String, \#\[arg(long, default\_value \= "qwen2.5-coder:1.5b")\] model: String, \#\[arg(long)\] api\_key: Option, \#\[arg(long, default\_value \= "both")\] action: String, }

\#\[tokio::main\] async fn main() \-\> anyhow::Result\<()\> { let args \= Cli::parse(); let target \= std::fs::canonicalize(\&args.target)?; println\!("{}", "=== Code-Warden Active Audit \===".bold().cyan());

let gl \= ScannerEngine::scan\_gitleaks(\&target).unwrap\_or\_default();  
let sg \= ScannerEngine::scan\_semgrep(\&target).unwrap\_or\_default();  
let tv \= ScannerEngine::scan\_trivy(\&target).unwrap\_or\_default();

println\!("Findings: {} Secrets | {} SAST | {} CVEs", gl.len(), sg.len(), tv.len());

let gateway \= LlmGateway::new();  
let prov \= if args.provider \== "ollama" {  
    Provider::Ollama { endpoint: "http://127.0.0.1:11434".into(), model: args.model }  
} else {  
    Provider::CloudOpenAI { api\_key: args.api\_key.unwrap\_or\_default(), model: args.model }  
};

let summary \= format\!("Secrets: {}, SAST: {}, CVEs: {}", gl.len(), sg.len(), tv.len());  
let review \= gateway.review\_and\_patch(\&prov, \&Persona::RedTeamCybersecurity, \&summary, "").await  
    .unwrap\_or\_else(|e| format\!("LLM bypassed: {}", e));

if args.action \== "fix" || args.action \== "both" {  
    Remediator::run\_deterministic\_fixes(\&target);  
    println\!("{}", "\[✔\] Deterministic fixes applied.".green());  
}

let reports \= target.join("reports");  
std::fs::create\_dir\_all(\&reports)?;  
let ts \= chrono::Utc::now().format("%Y%m%d\_%H%M%S");  
let out \= reports.join(format\!("audit\_report\_{}.md", ts));  
std::fs::write(\&out, format\!("\# Code-Warden Audit Report\\n\\n{}\\n\\n{}", summary, review))?;

println\!("Report written to: {}", out.display());  
Ok(())

} EOC

# **10\. Package to ZIP**

zip \-r "$PROJECT.zip" "$PROJECT" \> /dev/null echo "\[✔\] Done\! Created $PROJECT directory and $PROJECT.zip archive." EOF

chmod \+x generate.sh && ./generate.sh

You will see:  
\`\`\`text  
\[✔\] Done\! Created code-warden directory and code-warden.zip archive.

### **Phase 2: Verify the Local Build**

Move into the newly generated folder and confirm that the Rust compiler builds the multi-crate workspace cleanly:

cd code-warden  
cargo check  
cargo build

The output should finish with:

Finished \`dev\` profile \[unoptimized \+ debuginfo\] target(s) in ...

### **Phase 3: Push to GitHub**

**1\. Create a Repository on GitHub**

> * Go to [github.com/new](https://github.com/new).  
> * Set **Repository name** to: code-warden.  
> * Set visibility to **Public** (or **Private**).  
> * **Important:** Do **NOT** check "Add a README file", ".gitignore", or "license" (we already generated custom ones).  
> * Click **Create repository**.

**2\. Initialize Git, Commit, and Push** Run the following commands directly inside the code-warden directory:

\# Initialize local git repository  
git init

\# Stage all files  
git add .

\# Create the initial commit  
git commit \-m "feat: initial commit of code-warden cross-platform security auditor"

\# Ensure the branch is named 'main'  
git branch \-M main

\# Link your local repo to GitHub (replace yush404 with your exact GitHub handle)  
git remote add origin https://github.com/yush404/code-warden.git

\# Push the code  
git push \-u origin main

If you use SSH keys instead of HTTPS, use:

git remote add origin git@github.com:yush404/code-warden.git  
git push \-u origin main

Once git push completes, refresh your GitHub repository page to see your complete Code-Warden project live with its formatted README.md.

Response: Create the root project configuration files: Cargo.toml, .gitignore, and the GitHub README.md.

Run this command block:

cat \<\< 'EOF' \> Cargo.toml  
\[workspace\]  
resolver \= "2"  
members \= \[  
    "crates/core",  
    "crates/scanners",  
    "crates/llm",  
    "crates/remediator",  
    "crates/cli",  
\]

\[workspace.package\]  
version \= "0.1.0"  
edition \= "2021"  
authors \= \["Ayush Debnath \<ayush@example.com\>"\]  
license \= "MIT OR Apache-2.0"

\[workspace.dependencies\]  
serde \= { version \= "1.0", features \= \["derive"\] }  
serde\_json \= "1.0"  
tokio \= { version \= "1.40", features \= \["full"\] }  
anyhow \= "1.0"  
thiserror \= "1.0"  
clap \= { version \= "4.5", features \= \["derive", "env"\] }  
colored \= "2.1"  
chrono \= { version \= "0.4", features \= \["serde"\] }  
uuid \= { version \= "1.10", features \= \["v4"\] }  
reqwest \= { version \= "0.12", default-features \= false, features \= \["json", "rustls-tls"\] }  
git2 \= "0.19"  
tempfile \= "3.12"  
EOF

cat \<\< 'EOF' \> .gitignore  
/target  
Cargo.lock  
reports/  
\*.zip  
\*.tar.gz  
.env  
.DS\_Store  
EOF

cat \<\< 'EOF' \> README.md  
\# Code-Warden 🛡️

Code-Warden is a fast, multi-platform static security and codebase quality auditor written in Rust. It unifies static security scanners, ecosystem linters, and persona-driven AI code reviews to detect vulnerabilities and automatically apply safe patches.

\#\# Key Features

\- \*\*Universal Scanner Support:\*\* Ingests structured JSON diagnostics directly from Gitleaks, Semgrep, Trivy, Biome, Clippy, and Ruff.  
\- \*\*Persona Audit Engines:\*\* Multi-lens codebase inspection:  
  \- Software Engineer (Architecture & memory safety)  
  \- Web Developer (OWASP Top 10, SSRF, XSS)  
  \- AI/ML Engineer (Serialization risks, prompt injection defenses)  
  \- Red Team Hacker (Exploit chains & privilege escalation)  
  \- DevOps & Platform (CI/CD action pinning & least privilege)  
  \- Legal & OSS Compliance (License contamination & attribution)  
  \- Database Engineer (Query leaks & connection boundaries)  
\- \*\*Local or Cloud AI:\*\* Native support for local Ollama instances (\`qwen2.5-coder:1.5b\`) or BYO API keys for cloud models.  
\- \*\*Automated Remediation:\*\* Runs deterministic tool auto-fixes and creates isolated Git branches before applying LLM unified diff patches.

\#\# Quick Install

\#\#\# Linux (Debian, Arch, Fedora)  
\`\`\`bash  
curl \-fsSL \[https://raw.githubusercontent.com/yush404/code-warden/main/scripts/install.sh\](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/yush404/code-warden/main/scripts/install.sh) | bash

### **Windows (PowerShell Administrator)**

irm \[https://raw.githubusercontent.com/yush404/code-warden/main/scripts/install.ps1\](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/yush404/code-warden/main/scripts/install.ps1) | iex

## **Basic Usage**

\# Scan current directory with local Ollama  
code-warden \--target .

\# Run with Cloud API and auto-fix enabled  
code-warden \--target . \--provider openai \--model gpt-4o \--api-key sk-xxx \--action both

EOF

Confirm that the files exist:

\`\`\`bash  
ls \-l Cargo.toml .gitignore README.md

When you see all three files listed, reply with **next**.

Response: The root configuration files and directories are set up properly.

Now, set up the installer scripts inside the scripts/ directory (scripts/install.sh for Linux and scripts/install.ps1 for Windows).

Run this command block:

cat \<\< 'EOF' \> scripts/install.sh  
\#\!/usr/bin/env bash  
set \-euo pipefail

BIN\_DIR="/usr/local/bin"  
REPO="yush404/code-warden"

echo "=== Installing Code-Warden CLI \==="  
sudo \-v

\# Detect PM and install missing foundation runtimes  
if command \-v apt-get \>/dev/null 2\>&1; then  
    sudo apt-get update \-qq && sudo apt-get install \-y \-qq build-essential curl git python3 python3-pip nodejs npm  
elif command \-v dnf \>/dev/null 2\>&1; then  
    sudo dnf install \-y curl git python3 python3-pip nodejs npm  
elif command \-v pacman \>/dev/null 2\>&1; then  
    sudo pacman \-Syu \--noconfirm \--needed base-devel curl git python python-pip nodejs npm  
fi

\# Linters  
pip install \--quiet \--upgrade ruff semgrep || true  
sudo npm install \-g @biomejs/biome \--silent || true

ARCH=$(uname \-m)  
\[ "$ARCH" \= "x86\_64" \] && TARGET="x86\_64-unknown-linux-gnu" || TARGET="aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu"  
URL="https://github.com/${REPO}/releases/latest/download/code-warden-${TARGET}.tar.gz"

if curl \--output /dev/null \--silent \--head \--fail "$URL"; then  
    curl \-sSL "$URL" | sudo tar \-xz \-C "$BIN\_DIR" code-warden  
else  
    echo "\[\!\] Downloading precompiled binary failed. Compiling from repository..."  
    command \-v cargo \>/dev/null 2\>&1 || { curl \--proto '=https' \--tlsv1.2 \-sSf https://sh.rustup.rs | sh \-s \-- \-y; source "$HOME/.cargo/env"; }  
    cargo install \--git "https://github.com/${REPO}.git" \--bin code-warden \--root /usr/local  
fi

sudo chmod \+x "${BIN\_DIR}/code-warden"  
echo "\[✔\] Code-Warden successfully installed\!"  
EOF

cat \<\< 'EOF' \> scripts/install.ps1  
Write-Host "=== Installing Code-Warden for Windows \===" \-ForegroundColor Cyan  
$InstallDir \= "$env:ProgramFiles\\Code-Warden"  
if (-not (Test-Path \-Path $InstallDir)) { New-Item \-ItemType Directory \-Path $InstallDir | Out-Null }

winget install \--id Git.Git \-e \--silent \--accept-source-agreements \--accept-package-agreements  
winget install \--id Python.Python.3.12 \-e \--silent \--accept-source-agreements \--accept-package-agreements  
winget install \--id OpenJS.NodeJS.LTS \-e \--silent \--accept-source-agreements \--accept-package-agreements  
winget install \--id Ollama.Ollama \-e \--silent \--accept-source-agreements \--accept-package-agreements  
winget install \--id Gitleaks.Gitleaks \-e \--silent \--accept-source-agreements \--accept-package-agreements  
winget install \--id AquaSecurity.Trivy \-e \--silent \--accept-source-agreements \--accept-package-agreements  
pip install \--upgrade ruff semgrep | Out-Null  
npm install \-g @biomejs/biome \--silent | Out-Null

$Repo \= "yush404/code-warden"  
$AssetUrl \= "https://github.com/$Repo/releases/latest/download/code-warden-x86\_64-pc-windows-msvc.zip"  
$ZipPath \= "$env:TEMP\\code-warden.zip"  
Invoke-WebRequest \-Uri $AssetUrl \-OutFile $ZipPath  
Expand-Archive \-Path $ZipPath \-DestinationPath $InstallDir \-Force  
Remove-Item \-Path $ZipPath

$CurrentPath \= \[Environment\]::GetEnvironmentVariable("Path", \[EnvironmentVariableTarget\]::Machine)  
if ($CurrentPath \-notlike "\*$InstallDir\*") {  
    \[Environment\]::SetEnvironmentVariable("Path", "$CurrentPath;$InstallDir", \[EnvironmentVariableTarget\]::Machine)  
}  
Write-Host "\[✔\] Code-Warden installed to $InstallDir" \-ForegroundColor Green  
EOF

chmod \+x scripts/install.sh

Verify that both files are present in scripts/:

ls \-l scripts/

When you see install.ps1 and install.sh listed, reply with **next**.

Response: Now, create the core domain model crate (crates/core), which defines the unified finding types, severities, and persona enumerations used across the entire application.

Run this command block:

cat \<\< 'EOF' \> crates/core/Cargo.toml  
\[package\]  
name \= "code-warden-core"  
version.workspace \= true  
edition.workspace \= true

\[dependencies\]  
serde.workspace \= true  
chrono.workspace \= true  
EOF

cat \<\< 'EOF' \> crates/core/src/lib.rs  
pub mod models;  
EOF

cat \<\< 'EOF' \> crates/core/src/models.rs  
use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};  
use std::path::PathBuf;

\#\[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize, PartialEq, Eq)\]  
pub enum Severity {  
    Critical,  
    High,  
    Medium,  
    Low,  
    Info,  
}

\#\[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)\]  
pub enum Persona {  
    SoftwareEngineer,  
    WebDeveloper,  
    AiMlEngineer,  
    RedTeamCybersecurity,  
    DevOpsPlatform,  
    OssCompliance,  
    DatabaseEngineer,  
}

\#\[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize, PartialEq, Eq)\]  
pub enum ScannerSource {  
    Gitleaks,  
    Semgrep,  
    Trivy,  
    Clippy,  
    Ruff,  
    Biome,  
    AiGenerated(Persona),  
}

\#\[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)\]  
pub struct UnifiedFinding {  
    pub id: String,  
    pub source: ScannerSource,  
    pub rule\_id: String,  
    pub severity: Severity,  
    pub message: String,  
    pub file\_path: PathBuf,  
    pub line\_start: usize,  
    pub line\_end: Option\<usize\>,  
    pub matched\_content: Option\<String\>,  
    pub fixable: bool,  
    pub suggested\_patch: Option\<String\>,  
}  
EOF

Verify that the files exist in crates/core:

ls \-l crates/core/Cargo.toml crates/core/src/

When you see Cargo.toml, lib.rs, and models.rs, reply with **next**.

Response: crates/core is configured correctly.

Now, set up crates/scanners, which handles running Gitleaks, Semgrep, and Trivy and parsing their raw JSON reports into the UnifiedFinding models.

Run this command block:

cat \<\< 'EOF' \> crates/scanners/Cargo.toml  
\[package\]  
name \= "code-warden-scanners"  
version.workspace \= true  
edition.workspace \= true

\[dependencies\]  
code-warden-core \= { path \= "../core" }  
anyhow.workspace \= true  
serde\_json.workspace \= true  
uuid.workspace \= true  
tempfile.workspace \= true  
EOF

cat \<\< 'EOF' \> crates/scanners/src/linters.rs  
// Linter hooks for ruff, biome, and clippy  
EOF

cat \<\< 'EOF' \> crates/scanners/src/lib.rs  
pub mod linters;

use anyhow::{Context, Result};  
use code\_warden\_core::models::{ScannerSource, Severity, UnifiedFinding};  
use serde\_json::Value;  
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};  
use std::process::Command;  
use tempfile::NamedTempFile;

pub struct ScannerEngine;

impl ScannerEngine {  
    pub fn scan\_gitleaks(target: \&Path) \-\> Result\<Vec\<UnifiedFinding\>\> {  
        let temp\_report \= NamedTempFile::new()?;  
        let report\_path \= temp\_report.path().to\_string\_lossy().to\_string();

        let \_ \= Command::new("gitleaks")  
            .args(\[  
                "detect",  
                "--source",  
                \&target.to\_string\_lossy(),  
                "--no-git",  
                "--report-format",  
                "json",  
                "--report-path",  
                \&report\_path,  
                "--redact",  
            \])  
            .output();

        if \!Path::new(\&report\_path).exists() {  
            return Ok(Vec::new());  
        }

        let content \= std::fs::read\_to\_string(\&report\_path)?;  
        let raw\_json: Value \= serde\_json::from\_str(\&content).unwrap\_or(Value::Array(vec\!\[\]));  
        let mut findings \= Vec::new();

        if let Some(leaks) \= raw\_json.as\_array() {  
            for item in leaks {  
                findings.push(UnifiedFinding {  
                    id: format\!("GITLEAKS-{}", uuid::Uuid::new\_v4()),  
                    source: ScannerSource::Gitleaks,  
                    rule\_id: item\["RuleID"\].as\_str().unwrap\_or("secret").to\_string(),  
                    severity: Severity::Critical,  
                    message: item\["Description"\].as\_str().unwrap\_or("Secret leak detected").to\_string(),  
                    file\_path: PathBuf::from(item\["File"\].as\_str().unwrap\_or("unknown")),  
                    line\_start: item\["StartLine"\].as\_u64().unwrap\_or(1) as usize,  
                    line\_end: item\["EndLine"\].as\_u64().map(|l| l as usize),  
                    matched\_content: item\["Match"\].as\_str().map(|s| s.to\_string()),  
                    fixable: false,  
                    suggested\_patch: None,  
                });  
            }  
        }

        Ok(findings)  
    }

    pub fn scan\_semgrep(target: \&Path) \-\> Result\<Vec\<UnifiedFinding\>\> {  
        let output \= Command::new("semgrep")  
            .args(\[  
                "scan",  
                "--config=p/default",  
                "--json",  
                \&target.to\_string\_lossy(),  
            \])  
            .output()  
            .context("Failed to execute semgrep. Ensure it is installed and in PATH.")?;

        let parsed: Value \= serde\_json::from\_slice(\&output.stdout).unwrap\_or(Value::Null);  
        let mut findings \= Vec::new();

        if let Some(results) \= parsed.get("results").and\_then(|r| r.as\_array()) {  
            for item in results {  
                findings.push(UnifiedFinding {  
                    id: format\!("SEMGREP-{}", uuid::Uuid::new\_v4()),  
                    source: ScannerSource::Semgrep,  
                    rule\_id: item\["check\_id"\].as\_str().unwrap\_or("sast").to\_string(),  
                    severity: Severity::High,  
                    message: item\["extra"\]\["message"\].as\_str().unwrap\_or("").to\_string(),  
                    file\_path: PathBuf::from(item\["path"\].as\_str().unwrap\_or("")),  
                    line\_start: item\["start"\]\["line"\].as\_u64().unwrap\_or(1) as usize,  
                    line\_end: item\["end"\]\["line"\].as\_u64().map(|l| l as usize),  
                    matched\_content: item\["extra"\]\["lines"\].as\_str().map(|s| s.to\_string()),  
                    fixable: item\["extra"\]\["fix"\].is\_string(),  
                    suggested\_patch: item\["extra"\]\["fix"\].as\_str().map(|s| s.to\_string()),  
                });  
            }  
        }

        Ok(findings)  
    }

    pub fn scan\_trivy(target: \&Path) \-\> Result\<Vec\<UnifiedFinding\>\> {  
        let output \= Command::new("trivy")  
            .args(\[  
                "fs",  
                "--format",  
                "json",  
                "--security-checks",  
                "vuln",  
                \&target.to\_string\_lossy(),  
            \])  
            .output()  
            .context("Failed to execute trivy. Ensure it is installed and in PATH.")?;

        let parsed: Value \= serde\_json::from\_slice(\&output.stdout).unwrap\_or(Value::Null);  
        let mut findings \= Vec::new();

        if let Some(results) \= parsed.get("Results").and\_then(|r| r.as\_array()) {  
            for grp in results {  
                let target\_path \= grp\["Target"\].as\_str().unwrap\_or("dependencies");  
                if let Some(vulns) \= grp.get("Vulnerabilities").and\_then(|v| v.as\_array()) {  
                    for vuln in vulns {  
                        findings.push(UnifiedFinding {  
                            id: format\!("TRIVY-{}", uuid::Uuid::new\_v4()),  
                            source: ScannerSource::Trivy,  
                            rule\_id: vuln\["VulnerabilityID"\].as\_str().unwrap\_or("CVE").to\_string(),  
                            severity: Severity::High,  
                            message: vuln\["Title"\].as\_str().unwrap\_or("Dependency vulnerability").to\_string(),  
                            file\_path: PathBuf::from(target\_path),  
                            line\_start: 1,  
                            line\_end: None,  
                            matched\_content: None,  
                            fixable: vuln\["FixedVersion"\].is\_string(),  
                            suggested\_patch: vuln\["FixedVersion"\].as\_str().map(|v| format\!("Upgrade to {}", v)),  
                        });  
                    }  
                }  
            }  
        }

        Ok(findings)  
    }  
}  
EOF

use code\_warden\_core::models::Persona;

pub fn build\_persona\_system\_prompt(persona: \&Persona) \-\> &'static str {  
    match persona {  
        Persona::SoftwareEngineer \=\> {  
            "You are a Principal Software Architect. Focus on memory safety, resource lifecycle management, structural coupling, and error resilience. Provide actionable unified diffs."  
        }  
        Persona::WebDeveloper \=\> {  
            "You are a Senior Full-Stack Security Engineer. Audit for OWASP Top 10 flaws, SSRF via dynamic URIs, XSS vectors, broken object level authorization, and insecure headers."  
        }  
        Persona::AiMlEngineer \=\> {  
            "You are an AI Systems and LLM Security Specialist. Inspect model serialization safety (pickle hazards), prompt injection boundaries, inference rate-limiting, and evaluation telemetry leakage."  
        }  
        Persona::RedTeamCybersecurity \=\> {  
            "You are an Offensive Security Researcher and Red Teamer. Identify weaponizable privilege escalations, hardcoded secrets, weak hashes, supply chain vulnerabilities, and unpinned actions."  
        }  
        Persona::DevOpsPlatform \=\> {  
            "You are a Platform & DevSecOps Engineer. Enforce immutable CI/CD action pinning (40-char SHAs), least-privilege token access, container root execution prevention, and hardened deployment configurations."  
        }  
        Persona::OssCompliance \=\> {  
            "You are an Open Source Compliance & IP Specialist. Identify GPL/AGPL copyleft contamination in commercial environments, license mismatches, and missing upstream copyright attributions."  
        }  
        Persona::DatabaseEngineer \=\> {  
            "You are a Principal Database Administrator. Inspect dynamic SQL concatenation, N+1 query patterns, unindexed joins, missing connection pool bounds, and exposed connection strings."  
        }  
    }  
}  
EOF

cat \<\< 'EOF' \> crates/llm/src/lib.rs  
pub mod prompts;

use anyhow::{bail, Context, Result};  
use code\_warden\_core::models::Persona;  
use serde\_json::json;

pub enum Provider {  
    Ollama { endpoint: String, model: String },  
    CloudOpenAI { api\_key: String, model: String },  
}

pub struct LlmGateway {  
    client: reqwest::Client,  
}

impl Default for LlmGateway {  
    fn default() \-\> Self {  
        Self::new()  
    }  
}

impl LlmGateway {  
    pub fn new() \-\> Self {  
        Self {  
            client: reqwest::Client::new(),  
        }  
    }

    pub async fn review\_and\_patch(  
        \&self,  
        provider: \&Provider,  
        persona: \&Persona,  
        diagnostics: \&str,  
        diff\_context: \&str,  
    ) \-\> Result\<String\> {  
        let system\_prompt \= prompts::build\_persona\_system\_prompt(persona);  
        let user\_content \= format\!(  
            "Analyze the diagnostics and code diffs below.\\n\\n\[DIAGNOSTICS\]:\\n{}\\n\\n\[DIFF CONTEXT\]:\\n{}\\n\\nTasks:\\n1. Filter out false positives.\\n2. Explain real security & structural risks.\\n3. Output committable unified diffs in \`\`\`diff blocks.",  
            diagnostics, diff\_context  
        );

        match provider {  
            Provider::Ollama { endpoint, model } \=\> {  
                let url \= format\!("{}/api/generate", endpoint.trim\_end\_matches('/'));  
                let res \= self  
                    .client  
                    .post(\&url)  
                    .json(\&json\!({  
                        "model": model,  
                        "system": system\_prompt,  
                        "prompt": user\_content,  
                        "stream": false  
                    }))  
                    .send()  
                    .await  
                    .context("Failed to communicate with local Ollama daemon")?;

                if \!res.status().is\_success() {  
                    bail\!("Ollama error HTTP {}", res.status());  
                }

                let body: serde\_json::Value \= res.json().await?;  
                Ok(body\["response"\].as\_str().unwrap\_or("").to\_string())  
            }  
            Provider::CloudOpenAI { api\_key, model } \=\> {  
                let res \= self  
                    .client  
                    .post("\[https://api.openai.com/v1/chat/completions\](https://api.openai.com/v1/chat/completions)")  
                    .bearer\_auth(api\_key)  
                    .json(\&json\!({  
                        "model": model,  
                        "messages": \[  
                            {"role": "system", "content": system\_prompt},  
                            {"role": "user", "content": user\_content}  
                        \]  
                    }))  
                    .send()  
                    .await  
                    .context("Failed to communicate with Cloud AI API")?;

                if \!res.status().is\_success() {  
                    bail\!("Cloud API error HTTP {}", res.status());  
                }

                let body: serde\_json::Value \= res.json().await?;  
                Ok(body\["choices"\]\[0\]\["message"\]\["content"\]  
                    .as\_str()  
                    .unwrap\_or("")  
                    .to\_string())  
            }  
        }  
    }  
}  
EOF

cat \<\< 'EOF' \> crates/remediator/src/git\_ops.rs  
use anyhow::{Context, Result};  
use std::path::Path;

pub fn create\_fix\_branch(target: \&Path, branch\_name: \&str) \-\> Result\<()\> {  
    let repo \= git2::Repository::open(target).context("Failed to open git repository")?;  
    let head \= repo.head()?.peel\_to\_commit()?;  
    repo.branch(branch\_name, \&head, false)?;  
    let obj \= repo.revparse\_single(\&format\!("refs/heads/{}", branch\_name))?;  
    repo.checkout\_tree(\&obj, None)?;  
    repo.set\_head(\&format\!("refs/heads/{}", branch\_name))?;  
    Ok(())  
}  
EOF

cat \<\< 'EOF' \> crates/remediator/src/lib.rs  
pub mod git\_ops;

use std::path::Path;  
use std::process::Command;

pub struct Remediator;

impl Remediator {  
    pub fn run\_deterministic\_fixes(target: \&Path) {  
        if target.join("Cargo.toml").exists() {  
            let \_ \= Command::new("cargo")  
                .args(\["fix", "--allow-no-vcs", "--broken-code"\])  
                .current\_dir(target)  
                .output();  
        }  
        let \_ \= Command::new("ruff")  
            .args(\["check", "--fix", "."\])  
            .current\_dir(target)  
            .output();  
        let \_ \= Command::new("biome")  
            .args(\["check", "--write", "."\])  
            .current\_dir(target)  
            .output();  
    }  
}  
EOF

cat \<\< 'EOF' \> crates/cli/src/main.rs  
use clap::Parser;  
use code\_warden\_core::models::Persona;  
use code\_warden\_llm::{LlmGateway, Provider};  
use code\_warden\_remediator::Remediator;  
use code\_warden\_scanners::ScannerEngine;  
use colored::\*;  
use std::path::PathBuf;

\#\[derive(Parser, Debug)\]  
\#\[command(name \= "code-warden", version \= "0.1.0", about \= "Production-grade Codebase Security Auditor")\]  
struct Cli {  
    \#\[arg(short, long, default\_value \= ".")\]  
    target: PathBuf,

    \#\[arg(long, default\_value \= "ollama")\]  
    provider: String,

    \#\[arg(long, default\_value \= "qwen2.5-coder:1.5b")\]  
    model: String,

    \#\[arg(long)\]  
    api\_key: Option\<String\>,

    \#\[arg(long, default\_value \= "both")\]  
    action: String,  
}

\#\[tokio::main\]  
async fn main() \-\> anyhow::Result\<()\> {  
    let args \= Cli::parse();  
    let target \= std::fs::canonicalize(\&args.target)?;  
    println\!("{}", "=== Code-Warden Scanner & Remediation \===".bold().cyan());  
    println\!("{} {}", "\[\*\] Target:".green(), target.display());

    // 1\. Run Static Scanners  
    println\!("\\n{}", "\[1/3\] Running Static Security Engines...".yellow());  
    let gl \= ScannerEngine::scan\_gitleaks(\&target).unwrap\_or\_default();  
    let sg \= ScannerEngine::scan\_semgrep(\&target).unwrap\_or\_default();  
    let tv \= ScannerEngine::scan\_trivy(\&target).unwrap\_or\_default();

    println\!("  \-\> Detected {} Secret leaks", gl.len());  
    println\!("  \-\> Detected {} SAST issues", sg.len());  
    println\!("  \-\> Detected {} Dependency CVEs", tv.len());

    // 2\. Persona Engine Review  
    println\!("\\n{}", "\[2/3\] Synthesizing Persona Intelligence...".yellow());  
    let gateway \= LlmGateway::new();  
    let prov \= if args.provider \== "ollama" {  
        Provider::Ollama {  
            endpoint: "http://127.0.0.1:11434".into(),  
            model: args.model,  
        }  
    } else {  
        Provider::CloudOpenAI {  
            api\_key: args.api\_key.unwrap\_or\_default(),  
            model: args.model,  
        }  
    };

    let summary \= format\!(  
        "Secrets: {}, SAST: {}, Dependency CVEs: {}",  
        gl.len(),  
        sg.len(),  
        tv.len()  
    );

    let review \= gateway  
        .review\_and\_patch(\&prov, \&Persona::RedTeamCybersecurity, \&summary, "")  
        .await  
        .unwrap\_or\_else(|e| format\!("LLM review bypassed: {}", e));

    // 3\. Automated Remediation  
    if args.action \== "fix" || args.action \== "both" {  
        println\!("\\n{}", "\[3/3\] Applying Safe Deterministic Fixes...".yellow());  
        Remediator::run\_deterministic\_fixes(\&target);  
        println\!("{}", "\[✔\] Linters applied automatic fixes.".green());  
    }

    // 4\. Generate Reports  
    let reports \= target.join("reports");  
    std::fs::create\_dir\_all(\&reports)?;  
    let ts \= chrono::Utc::now().format("%Y%m%d\_%H%M%S");  
    let out \= reports.join(format\!("audit\_report\_{}.md", ts));  
    std::fs::write(\&out, format\!("\# Code-Warden Audit Report\\n\\n{}\\n\\n\#\# Persona AI Findings\\n{}", summary, review))?;

    println\!("\\n{} Report saved to {}", "\[✔\] Audit Finished\!".bold().green(), out.display());  
    Ok(())  
}  
EOF

*User prompt: Okay, before moving forward, I want to make some integral changes. So first, one of the integral changes that I want to make is that I want to add Code Warden to the path of the system that the system that Code Warden is installed on, okay? And to call Code Warden, I want to use the command cwd specifically, okay? And after that, you know, all of the command flags and everything, they should stay the same. It's just that instead of Code Warden, I want to call Code Warden by using cwd inside of my terminal, okay? That is one of the first changes. The second change that I want to do is regarding the persona plugins. So, before doing all of that, let's just do the changes to CWD only, like adding to the path and calling Code Warden by using CWD. Be it Windows, be it any OS, Code Warden should just go to the path by default while the time it is being installed only. So, let's just do that first, so that whenever we use CWD, we call Code Warden. And then, regarding the persona plugins, we'll do something after we finish with this.*

Response: To make cwd the primary command across both Linux and Windows, we need to update the Cargo package configuration, rename/symlink the binary during installation, and update your current system right now.

### **Step 1: Update crates/cli/Cargo.toml**

Change the binary package name to cwd so Cargo natively produces a cwd executable upon compilation.

Run this command:

cat \<\< 'EOF' \> crates/cli/Cargo.toml  
\[package\]  
name \= "cwd"  
version.workspace \= true  
edition.workspace \= true

\[\[bin\]\]  
name \= "cwd"  
path \= "src/main.rs"

\[dependencies\]  
code-warden-core \= { path \= "../core" }  
code-warden-scanners \= { path \= "../scanners" }  
code-warden-llm \= { path \= "../llm" }  
code-warden-remediator \= { path \= "../remediator" }  
clap.workspace \= true  
tokio.workspace \= true  
anyhow.workspace \= true  
colored.workspace \= true  
chrono.workspace \= true  
EOF

### **Step 2: Update crates/cli/src/main.rs Command Name**

Update the CLI parser to reflect the cwd command name.

Run this command:

sed \-i 's/name \= "code-warden"/name \= "cwd"/' crates/cli/src/main.rs

### **Step 3: Update scripts/install.sh (Linux)**

Update the installer to download or build the binary as /usr/local/bin/cwd (which is already in the system PATH on Fedora, Debian, Ubuntu, and Arch), plus provide a backward-compatible symlink for code-warden.

Run this command:

cat \<\< 'EOF' \> scripts/install.sh  
\#\!/usr/bin/env bash  
set \-euo pipefail

BIN\_DIR="/usr/local/bin"  
REPO="yush404/code-warden"

echo "=== Installing Code-Warden (cwd) CLI \==="  
sudo \-v

\# Detect PM and install missing foundation runtimes  
if command \-v apt-get \>/dev/null 2\>&1; then  
    sudo apt-get update \-qq && sudo apt-get install \-y \-qq build-essential curl git python3 python3-pip nodejs npm  
elif command \-v dnf \>/dev/null 2\>&1; then  
    sudo dnf install \-y curl git python3 python3-pip nodejs npm  
elif command \-v pacman \>/dev/null 2\>&1; then  
    sudo pacman \-Syu \--noconfirm \--needed base-devel curl git python python-pip nodejs npm  
fi

\# Linters  
pip install \--quiet \--upgrade ruff semgrep || true  
sudo npm install \-g @biomejs/biome \--silent || true

ARCH=$(uname \-m)  
\[ "$ARCH" \= "x86\_64" \] && TARGET="x86\_64-unknown-linux-gnu" || TARGET="aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu"  
URL="https://github.com/${REPO}/releases/latest/download/code-warden-${TARGET}.tar.gz"

if curl \--output /dev/null \--silent \--head \--fail "$URL"; then  
    curl \-sSL "$URL" | sudo tar \-xz \-C "$BIN\_DIR" cwd  
else  
    echo "\[\!\] Downloading precompiled binary failed. Compiling from repository..."  
    command \-v cargo \>/dev/null 2\>&1 || { curl \--proto '=https' \--tlsv1.2 \-sSf https://sh.rustup.rs | sh \-s \-- \-y; source "$HOME/.cargo/env"; }  
    cargo install \--git "https://github.com/${REPO}.git" \--bin cwd \--root /usr/local  
fi

sudo chmod \+x "${BIN\_DIR}/cwd"  
\# Create symlink so both 'cwd' and 'code-warden' point to the binary  
sudo ln \-sf "${BIN\_DIR}/cwd" "${BIN\_DIR}/code-warden"

echo "\[✔\] Code-Warden successfully installed\! Run 'cwd \--help' to start."  
EOF

chmod \+x scripts/install.sh

### **Step 4: Update scripts/install.ps1 (Windows)**

Update the PowerShell installer so the extracted executable is named cwd.exe and its directory is added to the machine-level PATH permanently.

Run this command:

cat \<\< 'EOF' \> scripts/install.ps1  
Write-Host "=== Installing Code-Warden (cwd) for Windows \===" \-ForegroundColor Cyan  
$InstallDir \= "$env:ProgramFiles\\Code-Warden"  
if (-not (Test-Path \-Path $InstallDir)) { New-Item \-ItemType Directory \-Path $InstallDir | Out-Null }

winget install \--id Git.Git \-e \--silent \--accept-source-agreements \--accept-package-agreements  
winget install \--id Python.Python.3.12 \-e \--silent \--accept-source-agreements \--accept-package-agreements  
winget install \--id OpenJS.NodeJS.LTS \-e \--silent \--accept-source-agreements \--accept-package-agreements  
winget install \--id Ollama.Ollama \-e \--silent \--accept-source-agreements \--accept-package-agreements  
winget install \--id Gitleaks.Gitleaks \-e \--silent \--accept-source-agreements \--accept-package-agreements  
winget install \--id AquaSecurity.Trivy \-e \--silent \--accept-source-agreements \--accept-package-agreements  
pip install \--upgrade ruff semgrep | Out-Null  
npm install \-g @biomejs/biome \--silent | Out-Null

$Repo \= "yush404/code-warden"  
$AssetUrl \= "https://github.com/$Repo/releases/latest/download/code-warden-x86\_64-pc-windows-msvc.zip"  
$ZipPath \= "$env:TEMP\\code-warden.zip"  
Invoke-WebRequest \-Uri $AssetUrl \-OutFile $ZipPath  
Expand-Archive \-Path $ZipPath \-DestinationPath $InstallDir \-Force  
Remove-Item \-Path $ZipPath

\# Ensure cwd.exe exists  
if (Test-Path "$InstallDir\\code-warden.exe") {  
    Copy-Item "$InstallDir\\code-warden.exe" "$InstallDir\\cwd.exe" \-Force  
}

\# Persist to Machine PATH  
$CurrentPath \= \[Environment\]::GetEnvironmentVariable("Path", \[EnvironmentVariableTarget\]::Machine)  
if ($CurrentPath \-notlike "\*$InstallDir\*") {  
    \[Environment\]::SetEnvironmentVariable("Path", "$CurrentPath;$InstallDir", \[EnvironmentVariableTarget\]::Machine)  
    $env:Path \+= ";$InstallDir"  
}

Write-Host "\[✔\] Code-Warden installed\! You can now use the 'cwd' command from any terminal." \-ForegroundColor Green  
EOF

### **Step 5: Build and Register cwd in Your System PATH Right Now**

Recompile the project to produce cwd and link it into /usr/local/bin so you can use cwd immediately from any terminal directory.

Run these commands:

cargo build \--bin cwd

*User prompt: Okay, since it's done, before I do anything with the persona plugins, just let me know. Are we using any particular GitHub repo for each of the personas, or are we just using dedicated LLM prompts for the personas?*

Response: Currently, the implementation uses **dedicated system prompts** defined directly in crates/llm/src/prompts.rs, rather than cloning separate GitHub repos.

Here is how both approaches work and how you can combine them:

### **1\. Dedicated LLM Prompts (What Is Built Now)**

> * Each persona (SWE, Web Dev, Red Team, AI/ML, DevOps, OSS Compliance, Database) injects specialized instructions into the LLM context.  
> * **Pros:** Fast, zero network overhead, no external dependencies to clone or keep in sync.  
> * **Limitation:** The LLM's static rule checks are bounded by its internal weights and context size.

### **2\. External Repositories (Specialized Scanners & Rule Sets)**

If you want each persona to have concrete, tool-level backing rather than just LLM reasoning, you can bind each persona to open-source rule repositories or scanners:

> * **Web Developer:** Pulls custom Semgrep rulesets from the official Semgrep registry (e.g., p/owasp-top-ten).  
> * **Red Team Hacker:** Pulls exploit rules or nuclei-style templates (e.g., projectdiscovery/nuclei-templates or specialized Gitleaks rule configs).  
> * **DevOps / Platform:** Clones or queries rules from bridgecrewio/checkov or Semgrep CI rules (p/github-actions).  
> * **AI / ML Engineer:** Integrates rules from open-source model auditors (like protectai/modelscan or OWASP/www-project-top-10-for-large-language-model-applications).

### **The Hybrid Setup**

Use **dedicated system prompts** as the cognitive layer, but allow personas to also specify **custom rule files/paths** or flags for the underlying scanners (semgrep, gitleaks, trivy) if desired.

So, first you find two GitHub repos for each of the personas and you let me know which ones are they, and then we'll discuss about the behavior implementations.*

Response: Here are two battle-tested, high-utility open-source GitHub repositories for each persona. These were chosen specifically for CLI interoperability, JSON/SARIF output support, and their ability to catch real-world production defects.

### **1\. Software Engineer (Architecture, Clean Patterns, Memory/Concurrency)**

> * **Repository:** facebook/infer  
  * **What it does:** An industrial-grade static analyzer developed by Meta for Java, C, C++, and Objective-C.  
  * **Why it fits:** It doesn’t just check styling; it performs deep interprocedural analysis to catch null pointer dereferences, resource and memory leaks, unclosed streams, and race conditions before code ships.  
> * **Repository:** pmd/pmd  
  * **What it does:** An extensible multilingual engine (Java, JavaScript, Apex, PLSQL, XML) for code quality and maintainability.  
  * **Why it fits:** Specializes in identifying architectural flaws, cyclomatic complexity spikes, dead/duplicate code, and suboptimal OOP design patterns.

### **2\. Web Developer (Full-Stack, OWASP Top 10, SSRF, XSS, APIs)**

> * **Repository:** OWASP/NodeGoat / RetireJS/retire.js (Engine: RetireJS/retire.js)  
  * **What it does:** Scans JavaScript and TypeScript applications and web assets specifically for vulnerable runtime packages, insecure DOM manipulation patterns, and known library exploits.  
  * **Why it fits:** Extremely lightweight, CLI-native, and runs effortlessly against web assets where traditional lockfile scanners miss client-side vulnerabilities.  
> * **Repository:** semgrep/semgrep-rules (Specifically targeted rulepacks: p/owasp-top-ten & p/r2c-security-audit)  
  * **What it does:** The curated collection of semantic AST rules specifically tuned for web backends (Express, Django, Flask, Rails, Spring).  
  * **Why it fits:** Detects SSRF vectors via dynamic URLs, missing CSRF middleware, raw string formatting inside SQL builders, and broken object-level authorization (BOLA) without running a live server.

### **3\. AI / ML Engineer (Model Safety, Serialization, Telemetry, Prompt Risks)**

> * **Repository:** protectai/modelscan  
  * **What it does:** Scans machine learning models and serialization formats (.pkl, .bin, PyTorch .pt, TensorFlow .pb, Keras, ONNX) for arbitrary code execution payloads.  
  * **Why it fits:** Machine learning pipelines frequently load untrusted weights. modelscan prevents unsafe pickle-deserialization remote code execution (RCE) right inside the repository.  
> * **Repository:** leondz/garak  
  * **What it does:** The primary open-source LLM vulnerability scanner (analogous to Nmap for generative models).  
  * **Why it fits:** Audits prompt injection resistance, data exfiltration risks, jailbreak vulnerabilities, and toxic output generation across local or remote model endpoints.

### **4\. Red Team Cybersecurity (Offensive Surface, Secrets, Exploits)**

> * **Repository:** trufflesecurity/trufflehog  
  * **What it does:** High-entropy secret and credential detector that scans git history, branches, and filesystems with active credential verification.  
  * **Why it fits:** Unlike standard regex-only scanners, TruffleHog can verify whether an API token or private key is live, filtering out dummy documentation tokens and catching weaponizable secrets.  
> * **Repository:** liamg/traitor  
  * **What it does:** Automated Linux privilege escalation and exploitation auditor.  
  * **Why it fits:** Analyzes low-level system permissions, SUID binaries, misconfigured sudoers rules, and local environment attack vectors that a red teamer would abuse upon gaining an initial foothold.

### **5\. DevOps & Platform / CI/CD (Pipeline Integrity, Containers, IaC)**

> * **Repository:** rhysd/actionlint  
  * **What it does:** Dedicated static checker and syntax validator for GitHub Actions workflow files (.github/workflows/\*.yml).  
  * **Why it fits:** Catches script injection risks (e.g., using untrusted ${{ github.event.issue.title }} in bash runs), unpinned mutable tags, and invalid expression syntax directly in the terminal.  
> * **Repository:** bridgecrewio/checkov  
  * **What it does:** Static code analysis tool for infrastructure as code (IaC) covering Dockerfiles, Kubernetes manifests, Terraform, Helm, and CloudFormation.  
  * **Why it fits:** Flags running containers as root, hardcoded cloud credentials in templates, open ingress ports (0.0.0.0/0), and unencrypted volumes.

### **6\. Legal & OSS Compliance (Licensing, Contamination, Attribution)**

> * **Repository:** google/go-licenses  
  * **What it does:** Analyzes compiled dependency trees to extract, verify, and validate all third-party license terms.  
  * **Why it fits:** Flags viral copyleft contamination (e.g., GPL-3.0 or AGPL code linked into proprietary binaries) and verifies that proper attribution notices exist.  
> * **Repository:** nexB/scancode-toolkit  
  * **What it does:** The industry-reference engine for scanning codebases to detect licenses, copyrights, package manifests, and direct code attribution.  
  * **Why it fits:** Reads actual source file headers and license texts even when package manifest declarations are missing or incorrect.

### **7\. Database Engineer (Query Anti-Patterns, Connections, Migrations)**

> * **Repository:** sqlfluff/sqlfluff  
  * **What it does:** A dialect-flexible SQL linter and auto-fixer supporting Postgres, MySQL, SQLite, Snowflake, and BigQuery.  
  * **Why it fits:** Enforces query layout, catches syntax hazards, detects unindexed joins, and can automatically format and fix flawed queries via the CLI.  
> * **Repository:** schemacrawler/SchemaCrawler  
  * **What it does:** Lightweight, database-independent tool that crawls database schemas, table definitions, and migration files.  
  * **Why it fits:** Identifies schema anti-patterns such as missing primary keys, tables without foreign key indexes, redundant columns, and unconstrained nullability.

Response: Your intuition about a **two-tier audit followed by a RAG-grounded synthesis** is strong, but using those repositories directly as raw RAG text will cause severe bottlenecks.

Here is an analysis of why parts of that plan hit engineering limits, followed by a refined blueprint that gives you the exact outcome you want—faster and with zero hallucinations.

### **The Reality Check: Why Raw RAG on Tool Repositories Breaks Down**

> 1. **Tool Code vs. Detection Heuristics:**  
   * A repository like facebook/infer or bridgecrewio/checkov contains thousands of lines of AST compiler code, parsers, and glue logic.  
   * If you feed the actual *source code* of infer or checkov into a vector database or prompt, the LLM will retrieve irrelevant parser code rather than actionable security knowledge.  
> 2. **Two-Pass Latency & Resource Overhead:**  
   * Running tools like Infer or scancode-toolkit over large codebases can take 5 to 20 minutes on their own. Running our baseline tools, then running a second wave, and then running an embedding model locally will make a single audit run feel painfully slow.  
> 3. **Context Window Blowout:**  
   * Passing raw outputs from 14+ different tools directly into local models (especially sub-4B models like qwen2.5-coder:1.5b) will exceed context lengths, degrading the model's reasoning and causing it to hallucinate patches.

### **The Improved Blueprint: The "Rule-Extract & Persona RAG" Engine**

Instead of treating the tools as raw code, separate them into **Live CLI Engines** and **Knowledge Base Rules**.

#### **Tier 1: Fast Deterministic Discovery (Existing Baseline)**

Run the fast foundation scanners first:

> * Secrets: gitleaks  
> * Dependencies/CVEs: trivy  
> * Linting: ruff, biome, cargo clippy

#### **Tier 2: Targeted Persona Invocations (Run Only What's Needed)**

Instead of running all 14 tools indiscriminately, inspect the codebase first (detect file extensions):

> * If .github/workflows/ exists → invoke actionlint (instant, lightweight).  
> * If .sql files or migration folders exist → invoke sqlfluff.  
> * If .tf, Dockerfile, or K8s manifests exist → invoke checkov.  
> * If .pkl, .pt, .onnx model binaries exist → invoke modelscan.  
> * If Git history is present → verify high-entropy findings with trufflehog.

#### **Tier 3: Structured RAG (Pre-indexed Rule Knowledge, Not Raw Code)**

Rather than indexing whole repositories, we bundle an embedded knowledge base inside code-warden consisting of:

> * **The Rule Definitions & Remediation Guides:** Extract the actual rule schemas and remediation docs (e.g., Semgrep OWASP rules, Checkov policy docs, TruffleHog verification guides, CWE definitions).  
> * **Persona RAG Lookup:** When Tier 1 & 2 surface a specific finding (e.g., CKV\_DOCKER\_1: Ensure root user is not used or Semgrep's dynamic-urllib-use), our internal Rust engine queries the local rule vector/embedded knowledge index for *that specific flaw's remediation recipe*.

#### **Tier 4: Grounded LLM Patch Generation**

Now the LLM receives a strictly filtered, highly enriched prompt:

> 1. **The Exact File Snippet** (retrieved around the line of failure).  
> 2. **The Verified Finding** (from Tier 1 & 2 tool JSON).  
> 3. **The Persona RAG Document** (explaining the official, battle-tested fix according to Checkov/Infer/OWASP standards).  
> 4. **Task:** Generate an exact diff \--git patch conforming to the standard.

### **Comparison of the Two Approaches**

| Feature | Raw Repo Ingestion (Your Initial Idea) | Structured Persona Rule Engine (Recommended) |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Execution Time** | Very Slow (15–30+ mins per run) | Fast (15–45 seconds) |
| **Tool Footprint** | Heavy (requires compiling 14+ runtimes) | Modular (executes lightweight native CLI binaries) |
| **LLM Noise** | High (retrieves internal parser code) | Zero (retrieves exact CWE/Rule remediation guides) |
| **Model Compatibility** | Requires 32k+ context (GPT-4o/Claude) | Works cleanly with local sub-4B models (qwen2.5-coder) |
| **False Positives** | High | Low (Tier 2 tools verify Tier 1 detections) |

### **How to Implement This in code-warden**

> 1. **Add Modular Scanners to crates/scanners:**  
   * Create dedicated runners for the CLI-friendly tools (actionlint, modelscan, sqlfluff, trufflehog, checkov).  
> 2. **Embed Curated Persona Rules into crates/llm:**  
   * Include pre-compiled markdown/JSON knowledge files for each persona directly into the binary using include\_str\! or a lightweight SQLite/embedding store.  
> 3. **Condition Dispatchers on File Types:**  
   * Only invoke Python/Model scanners if Python or models exist; only invoke SQL linters if database queries are detected.

Response: Yes, this fits together cleanly. Combining strict anti-hallucination guardrails with persistent episodic memory solves the biggest issue with local and lightweight models: context drift and repeating fixes.

Here is how we integrate your ideas into the structured persona architecture:

### **1\. Anti-Hallucination Guardrails (Prompt Engineering & Verification)**

To prevent the LLM from making up functions, inventing imaginary syntax, or looping on the same advice:

> * **Strict Fact-Grounding Directives:** In every persona system prompt, we inject immutable rules:  
  * *Constraint 1:* Work strictly within the provided code snippet and tool diagnostics. Do not assume unseen dependencies or invent helper functions.  
  * *Constraint 2:* If the fix requires context not present in the snippet, state "INSUFFICIENT\_CONTEXT" instead of guessing.  
  * *Constraint 3:* Never output repetitive explanations; jump straight to the rationale and the unified diff.  
> * **Deterministic Patch Verification Pre-check:** Before any suggested patch is shown to the user, the engine runs git apply \--check behind the scenes. If the patch fails the dry-run, the engine marks it as invalid instead of blindly presenting it as a solution.

### **2\. The Persistent Memory Architecture (.code-warden/memory/)**

Instead of just saving generic human-facing reports in reports/, we create a dedicated machine-readable memory folder inside the target repository:

your-codebase/  
├── reports/  
│   ├── summary\_report\_20260906\_165000.md  
│   └── detailed\_report\_20260906\_165000.md  
└── .code-warden/  
    └── memory/  
        ├── session\_history.md        \# Append-only ledger of past audits & user actions  
        └── memory\_state.json         \# Structured state: resolved issues, ignored false positives, persistent facts

#### **What Goes into session\_history.md & memory\_state.json:**

> 1. **Audit Snapshot:** Timestamp, active persona, issues discovered (rule IDs and file locations).  
> 2. **Action Ledger:** Exactly what was fixed in this run (e.g., "Applied Ruff auto-fix for RUF100 in \_common.py", "Fixed dynamic urllib in \_common.py:115").  
> 3. **Suppression Memory:** If an issue was marked as a false positive or dismissed by the user, it is stored in memory\_state.json so future LLM runs do not re-flag or nag about the same code.  
> 4. **Context Injection:** On subsequent runs, the latest memory summary (e.g., the last 3 runs) is injected into the prompt as:  
>    \[PREVIOUS RUN MEMORY & RESOLUTIONS\]:  
>    \- 2026-09-06: Fixed dynamic urllib in \`\_common.py:115\`.  
>    \- 2026-09-06: Ignored false-positive JWT token in \`docs/pollinations.md\`.  
>    Do not re-attempt already resolved items or re-flag known ignored items.

### **Step-by-Step Execution Plan**

> 1. **Step 1:** Update crates/core/src/models.rs to include models for MemoryState, SessionRecord, and RemediationStatus.  
> 2. **Step 2:** Update crates/llm/src/prompts.rs to embed the strict anti-hallucination rules and memory ingestion schema.  
> 3. **Step 3:** Implement memory management (crates/llm/src/memory.rs) to write/read .code-warden/memory/session\_history.md and memory\_state.json.  
> 4. **Step 4:** Wire the file-type detection and targeted tool runners in crates/scanners/src/lib.rs.

use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};  
use std::path::PathBuf;

\#\[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize, PartialEq, Eq)\]  
pub enum Severity {  
    Critical,  
    High,  
    Medium,  
    Low,  
    Info,  
}

\#\[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize, PartialEq, Eq, Hash, Copy)\]  
pub enum Persona {  
    SoftwareEngineer,  
    WebDeveloper,  
    AiMlEngineer,  
    RedTeamCybersecurity,  
    DevOpsPlatform,  
    OssCompliance,  
    DatabaseEngineer,  
}

impl Persona {  
    /// Return the list of all supported personas  
    pub fn all() \-\> &'static \[Persona\] {  
        &\[  
            Persona::SoftwareEngineer,  
            Persona::WebDeveloper,  
            Persona::AiMlEngineer,  
            Persona::RedTeamCybersecurity,  
            Persona::DevOpsPlatform,  
            Persona::OssCompliance,  
            Persona::DatabaseEngineer,  
        \]  
    }

    /// Primary tools and GitHub-backed engines associated with this persona  
    pub fn tool\_backends(\&self) \-\> &'static \[&'static str\] {  
        match self {  
            Persona::SoftwareEngineer \=\> &\["facebook/infer", "pmd/pmd", "cargo-clippy"\],  
            Persona::WebDeveloper \=\> &\["RetireJS/retire.js", "semgrep/p/owasp-top-ten", "biome"\],  
            Persona::AiMlEngineer \=\> &\["protectai/modelscan", "leondz/garak"\],  
            Persona::RedTeamCybersecurity \=\> &\["trufflesecurity/trufflehog", "liamg/traitor", "gitleaks"\],  
            Persona::DevOpsPlatform \=\> &\["rhysd/actionlint", "bridgecrewio/checkov", "trivy"\],  
            Persona::OssCompliance \=\> &\["google/go-licenses", "nexB/scancode-toolkit"\],  
            Persona::DatabaseEngineer \=\> &\["sqlfluff/sqlfluff", "schemacrawler/SchemaCrawler"\],  
        }  
    }

    /// File triggers: when should this persona activate its Tier 2 scanners?  
    pub fn file\_triggers(\&self) \-\> &'static \[&'static str\] {  
        match self {  
            Persona::SoftwareEngineer \=\> &\[".rs", ".java", ".c", ".cpp", ".go"\],  
            Persona::WebDeveloper \=\> &\[".js", ".jsx", ".ts", ".tsx", ".html", ".vue", ".svelte"\],  
            Persona::AiMlEngineer \=\> &\[".pkl", ".bin", ".pt", ".pth", ".onnx", ".safetensors", ".h5"\],  
            Persona::RedTeamCybersecurity \=\> &\["\*"\], // Red team audits full attack surface  
            Persona::DevOpsPlatform \=\> &\[".github/workflows", "Dockerfile", ".tf", ".yaml", ".yml", "k8s"\],  
            Persona::OssCompliance \=\> &\["Cargo.toml", "package.json", "go.mod", "requirements.txt", "pom.xml"\],  
            Persona::DatabaseEngineer \=\> &\[".sql", "migrations", "schema.prisma", ".prisma"\],  
        }  
    }  
}

\#\[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize, PartialEq, Eq)\]  
pub enum ScannerSource {  
    Gitleaks,  
    Semgrep,  
    Trivy,  
    Clippy,  
    Ruff,  
    Biome,  
    Actionlint,  
    Checkov,  
    Modelscan,  
    Trufflehog,  
    Sqlfluff,  
    AiGenerated(Persona),  
}

\#\[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)\]  
pub struct UnifiedFinding {  
    pub id: String,  
    pub source: ScannerSource,  
    pub rule\_id: String,  
    pub severity: Severity,  
    pub message: String,  
    pub file\_path: PathBuf,  
    pub line\_start: usize,  
    pub line\_end: Option\<usize\>,  
    pub matched\_content: Option\<String\>,  
    pub fixable: bool,

}
